<tr><td>↳近 brief</td><td>/ˈbriːf/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>简短的，简洁的</td><td>a brief summary<br>简短的总结</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gave a brief account of the accident to the police.  他向警方简短叙述了事故经过。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 digest</td><td>/daɪˈdʒest/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>领会，概括；消化</td><td>digest the information<br>领会这些信息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Give me a moment to digest what you just said.  给我点时间消化你刚说的话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 recapitulation</td><td>/ˌrikəˌpɪtʃəˈleɪʃən/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>概述，要点重述</td><td>a brief recapitulation<br>简短的概述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She closed the lecture with a brief recapitulation of three key points.  她以三个要点的简短概述结束了讲座。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 roundup</td><td>/ˈraʊnˌdʌp/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>综述，摘要汇总</td><td>a news roundup<br>新闻综述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The paper prints a weekly roundup of local sports results.  这家报纸每周刊登一次本地体育赛果综述。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 summarization</td><td>/ˌsʌmərəˈzeɪʃən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>概括，总结</td><td>automatic text summarization<br>自动文本摘要</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report ends with a brief summarization of the findings.  报告以对研究发现的简短概括结尾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 epitome</td><td>/ɪˈpɪtəmi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>缩影，典型；摘要</td><td>the epitome of elegance<br>优雅的典范</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That small hotel is the epitome of quiet good taste.  那家小旅馆是安静品味的典范。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>synthesis</b></td><td>/ˈsɪnθəsɪs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>合成，综合</td><td>a synthesis of ideas<br>各种思想的综合</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His theory is a synthesis of ideas drawn from several disciplines.  他的理论是多个学科思想的综合。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 admixture</td><td>/ædˈmɪkstʃər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>混合物，掺和料</td><td>an admixture of clay<br>掺入的黏土</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The concrete contains an admixture that slows the drying process.  这种混凝土含有一种延缓干燥的掺和料。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 alloy</td><td>/ˈæˌlɔɪ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>合金；使（品质）降低，掺杂</td><td>an alloy of copper and tin<br>铜锡合金</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Bronze is an alloy of copper and tin used since ancient times.  青铜是自古就使用的铜锡合金。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 amalgamation</td><td>/əˌmælɡəˈmeɪʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>合并，混合</td><td>the amalgamation of firms<br>公司的合并</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The amalgamation of the two unions created a powerful bloc.  两个工会的合并形成了一个强大的集团。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 compound</td><td>/kəmˈpaʊnd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>混合，合成；使恶化</td><td>compound a medicine<br>配制药剂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pharmacist compounded an ointment from three simple ingredients.  药剂师用三种简单原料配制出一种药膏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conflation</td><td>/kənˈfleɪʃən/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>合并，混同</td><td>a conflation of two ideas<br>两种观念的混同</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The essay suffers from a conflation of law and morality.  这篇文章的问题在于把法律与道德混为一谈。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fusion</td><td>/ˈfjuːʒən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>融合，结合；熔合</td><td>a fusion of cultures<br>文化的融合</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their cooking is a fusion of Korean and Peruvian traditions.  他们的烹饪是韩国与秘鲁传统的融合。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 meld</td><td>/ˈmeld/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>融合，合并</td><td>meld into one<br>融为一体</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The choir's voices melded into a single warm sound.  合唱团的声音融合成一种温暖的声响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 analysis</td><td>/əˈnæləsɪs/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>分析，分解</td><td>careful chemical analysis<br>细致的化学分析</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Careful analysis of the sample revealed three unexpected compounds.  对样本的细致分析发现了三种意外的化合物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 taking apart</td><td>/ˈteɪkɪŋ əˈpɑrt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>拆解，分解</td><td>taking apart the engine<br>把发动机拆开</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Taking apart the old clock taught him more than any textbook.  拆解那台旧钟教给他的比任何教科书都多。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>tacit</b></td><td>/ˈtæsɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>默许的，不言而喻的</td><td>tacit approval<br>默许</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager gave tacit approval by simply saying nothing at all.  经理什么也不说，等于默许了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 implied</td><td>/ɪmˈplaɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>暗示的，含蓄的</td><td>an implied threat<br>暗含的威胁</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His polite note carried an implied threat that everyone recognized.  他礼貌的字条里含着人人都听得出的威胁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unexpressed</td><td>/ˌʌnɪkˈsprɛst/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>未说出的，未表露的</td><td>unexpressed doubts<br>未说出的疑虑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her unexpressed doubts surfaced only after the contract was signed.  她未说出的疑虑直到合同签署后才浮现。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unspoken</td><td>/ʌnˈspoʊkən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>未说出口的，心照不宣的</td><td>an unspoken agreement<br>不言而喻的约定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was an unspoken rule that nobody mentioned his first marriage.  有条不言而喻的规矩：没人提他的第一次婚姻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unvoiced</td><td>/ʌnˈvɔɪst/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>未表达出来的</td><td>unvoiced objections<br>未说出的反对</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Several unvoiced objections finally appeared in the written survey.  几项未说出口的反对最终出现在书面问卷中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wordless</td><td>/ˈwɜːrdləs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无言的，不用言语的</td><td>a wordless glance<br>无言的一瞥</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They exchanged a wordless glance and left the room.  他们交换了无言的一瞥便离开房间。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 explicit</td><td>/ɪkˈsplɪsɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>表达清晰的，明确的</td><td>explicit instructions<br>明确的指示</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave explicit instructions about how to store the samples.  她就样品的保存方式给出了明确指示。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 express</td><td>/ɪkˈspres/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>表达，表现；快速的</td><td>express deep gratitude<br>表达深切感谢</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She struggled to express the gratitude she felt toward her mentor.  她难以表达对导师的感激之情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 expressed</td><td>/ɪkˈsprest/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>明确表达的</td><td>the expressed wishes<br>明确表达的意愿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They acted against the clearly expressed wishes of the committee.  他们的行动违背了委员会明确表达的意愿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 spoken</td><td>/ˈspoʊkən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>口头说出的，口语的</td><td>a spoken agreement<br>口头协议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A spoken promise carried little weight without anything in writing.  没有书面记录，口头承诺没什么分量。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 stated</td><td>/ˈsteɪtɪd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>明确陈述的，规定的</td><td>the stated purpose<br>明确宣称的目的</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The stated purpose of the trip was research, not tourism.  这次出行明确宣称的目的是研究，而非旅游。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 voiced</td><td>/ˈvɔɪst/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>说出的，表达出来的</td><td>openly voiced concerns<br>公开表达的担忧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Openly voiced concerns forced the school to change its plan.  公开表达的担忧迫使学校改变了计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>taciturn</b></td><td>/ˈtæsɪtɜːrn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>沉默寡言的，话少的</td><td>a taciturn farmer<br>沉默寡言的农夫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The taciturn farmer answered our questions with a nod or a shrug.  沉默寡言的农夫只用点头或耸肩回答我们的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 laconic</td><td>/ləˈkɑːnɪk/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>简洁（以至于显得粗鲁或难以理解）的</td><td>a laconic reply<br>简短生硬的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His laconic reply told us nothing about what had actually happened.  他简短生硬的回答没有告诉我们实际发生了什么。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reserved</td><td>/rɪˈzɜːrvd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>内向的，缄默的</td><td>a quiet, reserved man<br>安静内向的男子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her father was a reserved man who rarely spoke about his feelings.  她的父亲是个内向的人，很少谈论自己的感受。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reticent</td><td>/ˈretɪsnt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>沉默不语的</td><td>reticent about his past<br>对往事讳莫如深</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was oddly reticent about the years he spent working abroad.  他对自己在国外工作的那些年出奇地沉默不语。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tight-lipped</td><td>/ˌtaɪt ˈlɪpt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>守口如瓶的，沉默寡言的</td><td>remain tight-lipped<br>始终守口如瓶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Officials remained tight-lipped about the cause of the delay.  官员们对延误原因始终守口如瓶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 uncommunicative</td><td>/ˌʌnkəˈmjuːnɪkətɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>沉默寡言的，不爱说话的</td><td>an uncommunicative teenager<br>沉默寡言的少年</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He grew uncommunicative after the move to a new school.  转到新学校后他变得沉默寡言。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 garrulous</td><td>/ˈɡærələs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>啰嗦的，话多令人厌烦的</td><td>a garrulous neighbor<br>唠叨的邻居</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our garrulous neighbor talked for an hour about his lawn.  我们唠叨的邻居就他的草坪讲了一个小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 loquacious</td><td>/ləˈkweɪʃəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>话多的</td><td>a loquacious guide<br>话多的导游</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our loquacious guide talked steadily from the museum door to the exit.  我们话多的导游从博物馆门口一直讲到出口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 glib</td><td>/ɡlɪb/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>油腔滑调的；肤浅的</td><td>a glib salesman<br>油腔滑调的销售员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His glib answers convinced no one on the hiring committee that afternoon.  那天下午他油腔滑调的回答没能说服招聘委员会的任何人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 expansive</td><td>/ɪkˈspænsɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>话多的，健谈的</td><td>in an expansive mood<br>谈兴正浓</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After dinner he grew expansive and told stories all evening.  晚饭后他变得健谈，整晚讲故事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 prolix</td><td>/ˈproʊlɪks/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>啰嗦的，冗长的</td><td>prolix legal prose<br>冗长的法律文字</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge criticized the prolix brief for burying its argument.  法官批评这份冗长的书状埋没了自己的论点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 voluble</td><td>/ˈvɑːljəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>健谈的，话多的</td><td>a voluble speaker<br>健谈的讲话者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our voluble guide talked cheerfully about every stone in the old city.  我们健谈的导游兴致勃勃地讲述古城里的每一块石头。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>tackle</b></td><td>/ˈtækəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>着手处理</td><td>tackle a difficult problem<br>着手解决难题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new committee will tackle a difficult problem that others avoided.  新委员会将着手处理别人回避的难题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dive into</td><td>/ˈdaɪv ˌɪntu/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>立即投入，一头扎入</td><td>dive into the work<br>立刻投入工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She dived into the project without waiting for further instructions.  她没等进一步指示就一头扎进了项目。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wade in</td><td>/ˈweɪd ˌɪn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>大胆着手，贸然介入</td><td>wade in without preparation<br>毫无准备就动手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He waded in and started rewriting the report immediately.  他二话不说就动手重写那份报告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 5

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>tact</b></td><td>/tækt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>机智；圆通；分寸感</td><td>handle it with tact<br>机敏得体地处理</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She handled the awkward question with remarkable tact and warmth.  她以极高的分寸和善意处理了这个尴尬问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 diplomacy</td><td>/dɪˈploʊməsi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>交际手腕，圆通</td><td>handle with diplomacy<br>圆通地处理</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took real diplomacy to refuse the offer without offending anyone.  拒绝这个提议而不冒犯任何人需要真正的手腕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 clumsiness</td><td>/ˈklʌmzinəs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>笨拙，不得体</td><td>social clumsiness<br>社交上的笨拙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His social clumsiness offended the guests within five minutes.  他社交上的笨拙五分钟内就得罪了客人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 insensitivity</td><td>/ˌɪnsensəˈtɪvəti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>冷漠，麻木不仁</td><td>insensitivity to criticism<br>对批评的麻木</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His insensitivity to other people's feelings cost him friends.  他对别人感受的麻木让他失去了朋友。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 tactlessness</del></td><td>/ˈtæktləsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不得体，不会应对</td><td>sheer tactlessness<br>十足的不得体</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His tactlessness at the funeral offended several relatives.  他在葬礼上的不得体冒犯了几位亲戚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>tactile</b></td><td>/ˈtæktəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有触觉的，能触知的</td><td>tactile sensation<br>触觉感受</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Blind readers rely on the tactile sensation of raised dots.  盲人读者依靠凸点带来的触觉感受。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>tactless</b></td><td>/ˈtæktləs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不机智的，笨拙的</td><td>a tactless comment<br>不得体的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One tactless comment about her age spoiled the whole evening.  一句关于她年龄的不得体评论毁了整个晚上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 graceless</td><td>/ˈɡreɪsləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>笨拙的，不优雅的</td><td>a graceless heavy stride<br>笨重不雅的步态</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His graceless attempt at an apology made things worse.  他笨拙的道歉尝试让事情更糟了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ill-advised</td><td>/ˌɪl ədˈvaɪzd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不明智的，欠考虑的</td><td>an ill-advised remark<br>欠考虑的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His ill-advised joke about her accent ruined the dinner.  他关于她口音的失当玩笑毁了这顿晚餐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imprudent</td><td>/ɪmˈpruːdnt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不明智的</td><td>an imprudent investment<br>不明智的投资</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Borrowing so much money at that rate was clearly imprudent.  以那样的利率借这么多钱显然是不明智的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 indelicate</td><td>/ɪnˈdelɪkət/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不雅的，粗俗的</td><td>an indelicate question<br>不雅的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Asking about her salary was an indelicate question at the party.  在聚会上问她的薪水是个不得体的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 injudicious</td><td>/ˌɪndʒuˈdɪʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不明智的，欠考虑的</td><td>an injudicious remark<br>欠考虑的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One injudicious comment cost him the whole negotiation.  一句欠考虑的话让他输掉了整场谈判。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 undiplomatic</td><td>/ˌʌndɪpləˈmætɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不圆通的，不得体的</td><td>an undiplomatic refusal<br>不得体的拒绝</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His undiplomatic email offended half the department at once.  他不得体的邮件一下惹恼了半个部门。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 advisable</td><td>/ədˈvaɪzəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的，可取的</td><td>it seems advisable<br>这似乎是明智的</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It is advisable to book your tickets several weeks early.  提前几周订票是明智的做法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 discreet</td><td>/dɪˈskriːt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（言行）谨慎的</td><td>a discreet inquiry<br>谨慎的询问</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A discreet inquiry into his background turned up nothing worth reporting.  对他背景的谨慎查访没查出任何值得上报的事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 judicious</td><td>/dʒuˈdɪʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的，慎重的</td><td>a judicious choice<br>明智的选择</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A judicious use of savings carried the family through two difficult years.  明智地使用积蓄让这家人度过了两个艰难的年头。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 prudent</td><td>/ˈpruːdnt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的；小心谨慎的，审慎的</td><td>a prudent investment<br>一笔审慎的投资</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It would be prudent to save part of every paycheck.  每份工资都存下一部分才是明智之举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tactful</td><td>/ˈtæktfəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>得体的，圆通的</td><td>a tactful reply<br>得体的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A tactful reply saved everyone from an awkward argument.  一个得体的回答使大家免于一场尴尬的争论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wise</td><td>/ˈwaɪz/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的，有智慧的</td><td>a wise decision<br>明智的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was wise of her to save money before quitting the job.  她辞职前先存钱，很明智。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>tilt</b></td><td>/tɪlt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>（使）倾斜；倾斜度</td><td>tilt the screen back<br>把屏幕向后倾斜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He tilted the screen back until the glare finally disappeared.  他把屏幕向后倾斜，直到反光终于消失。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 angle</td><td>/ˈæŋɡəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>使倾斜；角度</td><td>angle the mirror<br>把镜子调斜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He angled the lamp so the light fell on the page.  他把灯调斜，使光照在书页上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cant</td><td>/ˈkænt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>行话；伪善之言</td><td>political cant<br>政治套话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His speech was pure cant dressed up as principle.  他的演讲不过是伪装成原则的套话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 heel</td><td>/ˈhiːl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>（船）倾斜；脚跟</td><td>heel over in the wind<br>在风中倾斜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The yacht heeled sharply as the gust hit.  阵风袭来时游艇急剧倾斜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 list</td><td>/lɪst/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>（使）倾斜</td><td>the ship began to list<br>船开始倾斜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The overloaded ship began to list badly in the rising waves.  这艘超载的船在渐高的浪中开始严重倾斜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 incline</td><td>/ɪnˈklaɪn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>（使）倾斜；倾向</td><td>incline one's head<br>把头倾向一侧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She inclined her head slightly to hear him.  她微微侧头以便听清他说话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pitch</td><td>/ˈpɪtʃ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>（船、飞机）颠簸倾斜</td><td>pitch in heavy seas<br>在大浪中颠簸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The boat pitched so badly that dishes slid off.  船颠簸得厉害，盘子都滑落了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slant</td><td>/slænt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>（使）倾斜；斜度</td><td>the floor slants slightly<br>地面略微倾斜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old wooden floor slants slightly toward the far corner.  这块旧木地板向远处的角落略微倾斜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slope</td><td>/ˈsloʊp/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>倾斜；斜坡</td><td>slope gently downward<br>缓缓向下倾斜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawn slopes gently toward the river.  草坪缓缓向河边倾斜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tip</td><td>/ˈtɪp/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>（使）倾斜，倾倒</td><td>tip the chair back<br>把椅子向后倾</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not tip the glass or the tea will spill.  别把杯子倾斜，茶会洒出来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bend</td><td>/ˈbend/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>使弯曲；弯道</td><td>bend the metal rod<br>弯折金属棒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He tried to bend the metal rod to fit the narrow frame.  他试着把金属棒弯折，以便装进窄框。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grade</td><td>/ˈɡreɪd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>坡度，倾斜度</td><td>a steep grade<br>陡坡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Trucks slow to a crawl on the steep grade past the quarry.  卡车在采石场外的陡坡上慢得像爬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gradient</td><td>/ˈɡreɪdiənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>坡度，倾斜度</td><td>a gentle gradient<br>平缓的坡度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The path climbs at a gentle gradient all the way to the lake.  这条小路以平缓的坡度一直通到湖边。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inclination</td><td>/ˌɪnkləˈneɪʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>倾向，意愿；倾斜</td><td>an inclination to agree<br>倾向于同意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She had no inclination to argue with him that evening.  那天晚上她无意与他争辩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lean</td><td>/ˈliːn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>瘦的，精瘦的</td><td>lean and muscular<br>精瘦而结实</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of running left him lean and quick.  多年跑步让他精瘦而敏捷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 leaning</td><td>/ˈliːnɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>倾斜的；倾向</td><td>a leaning tower<br>倾斜的塔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The leaning wall was propped up with heavy timbers.  倾斜的墙用粗木支撑起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 erect</td><td>/ɪˈrekt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>直立的，垂直的</td><td>stand erect<br>笔直地站着</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old soldier still stood perfectly erect.  这位老兵仍站得笔直。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>taint</b></td><td>/teɪnt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使（品质）污损</td><td>taint someone's reputation<br>损害某人名誉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scandal tainted his reputation for the rest of his career.  这场丑闻在他余下的职业生涯中都损害着他的名声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blemish</td><td>/ˈblemɪʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>缺点，污点；损害，玷污</td><td>a blemish on his record<br>他履历上的污点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One late payment left a small blemish on his credit record.  一次逾期付款在他的信用记录上留下了小小的污点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 darken</td><td>/ˈdɑːrkən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>使变暗，变黑</td><td>darken the room<br>使房间变暗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy clouds darkened the valley by mid-afternoon.  厚云在午后使山谷变暗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mar</td><td>/mɑːr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>破坏，削弱</td><td>mar the celebration<br>破坏庆典气氛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A careless remark at dinner managed to mar an otherwise perfect evening.  餐桌上一句轻率的话毁掉了本该完美的一晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spoil</td><td>/ˈspɔɪl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>损坏，破坏；溺爱</td><td>spoil the surprise<br>破坏惊喜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy rain spoiled the picnic we had planned for weeks.  大雨破坏了我们筹划数周的野餐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tarnish</td><td>/ˈtɑːrnɪʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>玷污</td><td>tarnish one's reputation<br>玷污某人名声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One careless scandal tarnished a reputation built over thirty years.  一次不慎的丑闻玷污了三十年建立的名声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vitiate</td><td>/ˈvɪʃieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>削弱，损害；使无效</td><td>vitiate an agreement<br>使协议失效</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A single false statement can vitiate the entire contract.  一句虚假陈述就能使整份合同失效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pristine</td><td>/prɪˈstiːn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>崭新的；纯净的；原始的</td><td>pristine white snow<br>洁白无垢的雪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cabin sat beside a pristine lake untouched by development.  那间小屋坐落在一个未受开发影响的纯净湖泊旁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unspoiled</td><td>/ʌnˈspɔɪld/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>未受损的，未被破坏的</td><td>an unspoiled coastline<br>未受破坏的海岸线</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The island keeps an unspoiled coastline with no hotels at all.  这座岛保有一段完全没有旅馆的原始海岸线。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wholesome</td><td>/ˈhoʊlsəm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有益身心健康的</td><td>wholesome home-cooked food<br>有益健康的家常饭菜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children grew strong on wholesome food and plenty of fresh air.  孩子们靠有益健康的食物和充足的新鲜空气长得很结实。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unadulterated</td><td>/ˌʌnəˈdʌltəreɪtɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>纯净的，未掺杂的</td><td>unadulterated joy<br>纯粹的喜悦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her face showed unadulterated delight at the news.  听到这消息她脸上是纯粹的喜悦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>tamper</b></td><td>/ˈtæmpər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>篡改；擅自摆弄，干预</td><td>tamper with the evidence<br>篡改证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Someone had tampered with the evidence before the trial began.  开庭前有人篡改过证据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 diddle with</td><td>/ˈdɪdəl wɪð/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>胡乱摆弄，瞎调整</td><td>diddle with the settings<br>乱动设置</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Stop diddling with the settings or you will break the camera.  别再乱动设置，不然你会把相机弄坏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fiddle with</td><td>/ˈfɪdəl wɪð/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>摆弄，乱动</td><td>fiddle with the controls<br>乱动控制装置</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He fiddled with the radio dial until the static disappeared.  他摆弄收音机旋钮，直到杂音消失。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fool with</td><td>/ˈful wɪð/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>瞎弄，胡乱摆弄</td><td>fool with the wiring<br>瞎动电线</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Never fool with the wiring while the power is still on.  电源没断时千万不要瞎动电线。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mess with</td><td>/ˈmɛs wɪð/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>乱动，胡乱干预</td><td>mess with my files<br>乱动我的文件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Someone messed with my files and deleted half the folder.  有人乱动我的文件，删掉了半个文件夹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>tangent</b></td><td>/ˈtændʒənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>离题（的），不相关（的）</td><td>go off on a tangent<br>突然离题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speaker went off on a tangent about his childhood pets.  演讲者突然离题谈起了他童年的宠物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 excursive</del></td><td>/ɪkˈskɜːrsɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>离题的；散漫的</td><td>an excursive narrative<br>散漫的叙述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His excursive lecture wandered far from the announced topic.  他的讲座散漫离题，远离了预告的主题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 digressive</td><td>/daɪˈɡrɛsɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>离题的，枝节的</td><td>a digressive lecture<br>离题的讲座</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His digressive style made the short talk feel endless.  他离题的讲法让短短的讲话显得没完没了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 essential</td><td>/ɪˈsenʃəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>本质的，必不可少的</td><td>an essential quality<br>本质的特性</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Patience is an essential part of her character.  耐心是她性格中本质的一部分。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>tangible</b></td><td>/ˈtændʒəbl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可感知的</td><td>tangible benefits<br>实实在在的好处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Voters want tangible benefits, not vague promises about the future.  选民想要实实在在的好处，而不是对未来的空泛承诺。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 palpable</td><td>/ˈpælpəbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明显的，易觉察的</td><td>a palpable sense of relief<br>明显的宽慰感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A palpable tension filled the room as the verdict was read aloud.  宣读判决时，房间里弥漫着明显的紧张气氛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 touchable</td><td>/ˈtʌtʃəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可触摸的，可感知的</td><td>a touchable surface<br>可触摸的表面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The museum offers touchable models for visitors who cannot see.  博物馆为视障参观者提供可触摸的模型。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 impalpable</td><td>/ɪmˈpælpəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以察觉的，无形的</td><td>an impalpable difference<br>难以察觉的差别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A faint, impalpable tension hung over the negotiating table.  谈判桌上笼罩着一种难以察觉的紧张气氛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 intangible</td><td>/ɪnˈtændʒəbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无法感知的，无形的</td><td>intangible assets<br>无形资产</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A company's reputation is an intangible asset that no balance sheet captures.  公司声誉是一项资产负债表无法体现的无形资产。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unable to perceive</td><td>/ʌnˈeɪbəl tu pərˈsiv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无法感知的</td><td>unable to perceive the difference<br>无法察觉差别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Most listeners were unable to perceive any difference between the recordings.  多数听众无法察觉两份录音之间的任何差别。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>tangy</b></td><td>/ˈtæŋi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（味道）浓烈刺激的</td><td>a tangy lemon sauce<br>味道刺激的柠檬酱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She served the grilled fish with a tangy lemon sauce.  她给烤鱼配上味道刺激的柠檬酱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pungent</td><td>/ˈpʌndʒənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>（言辞）辛辣尖刻的；刺鼻的</td><td>pungent criticism<br>辛辣的批评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His pungent criticism of the report left everyone at the table silent.  他对这份报告辛辣的批评让全桌人都沉默了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 strong</td><td>/strɔːŋ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>强壮的，坚固的</td><td>strong arms<br>强壮的臂膀</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bridge is strong enough to carry heavy trucks.  这座桥足够坚固，能承载重型卡车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bland</td><td>/blænd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无趣的</td><td>a bland performance<br>平淡的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The critics called it a bland performance with no real feeling.  评论家称这是一场毫无真情、十分无趣的表演。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mild</td><td>/ˈmaɪld/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>温和的，不严厉的</td><td>a mild punishment<br>轻微的处罚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The winter here is unusually mild and rarely brings snow.  这里的冬天格外温和，很少下雪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 smooth</td><td>/ˈsmuːð/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>温和的，圆滑的；平滑的</td><td>a smooth talker<br>说话圆滑的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is a smooth talker who never offends anyone in negotiations.  他谈判时言辞圆滑，从不得罪任何人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>tantalize</b></td><td>/ˈtæntəlaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激起，挑逗，引诱</td><td>tantalize with promises<br>用承诺引诱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The smell of roast meat tantalized the hungry travelers.  烤肉的香味挑逗着饥饿的旅人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 comfort</td><td>/ˈkʌmfərt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>舒适；安慰</td><td>live in comfort<br>过舒适的生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old chair gave him a great deal of comfort.  那把旧椅子给他带来极大的舒适。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 console</td><td>/kənˈsoʊl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>安慰，慰藉</td><td>console a grieving friend<br>安慰悲伤的朋友</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nothing anyone said could console her that evening.  那天晚上无论谁说什么都无法安慰她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 solace</td><td>/ˈsɑːləs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>安慰</td><td>find solace in music<br>在音乐中找到安慰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She found solace in music during the long months of her illness.  在漫长的病中，她在音乐里找到安慰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 alleviate</td><td>/əˈliːvieɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>缓和，减轻</td><td>alleviate the pain<br>减轻疼痛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A warm bath helped alleviate the soreness in his back.  热水澡帮他缓解了背部的酸痛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 assuage</td><td>/əˈsweɪdʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>缓和，减轻；平息</td><td>assuage his fears<br>减轻他的恐惧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor's speech did little to assuage public anger.  市长的演讲几乎没能平息公众的怒火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 relieve</td><td>/rɪˈliːv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>减轻，缓解；换班替换</td><td>relieve the pressure<br>缓解压力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A short walk relieved the tension in her shoulders.  短暂散步缓解了她肩膀的紧张。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 satiate</td><td>/ˈseɪʃieɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>（使）饱足（的），过分满足（的）</td><td>satiate his curiosity<br>充分满足他的好奇心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No amount of reading could satiate the curiosity of that boy about stars.  再多的阅读也无法满足那男孩对星星的好奇。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 6

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>tantamount</b></td><td>/ˈtæntəmaʊnt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>等价的，与…相等的</td><td>tantamount to a refusal<br>等于是拒绝</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His long silence was tantamount to a refusal of our request.  他久久的沉默等于是拒绝了我们的请求。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 incommensurate</del></td><td>/ˌɪnkəˈmɛnsərɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不相称的，不成比例的</td><td>incommensurate with the effort<br>与付出不相称</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tiny reward was incommensurate with the years of work.  这点微薄的报酬与多年的付出不相称。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>tantrum</b></td><td>/ˈtæntrəm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>勃然大怒，发脾气</td><td>throw a tantrum<br>大发脾气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tired child threw a tantrum in the middle of the store.  疲倦的孩子在商店中央大发脾气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blowup</td><td>/ˈbloʊˌʌp/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>发脾气，爆发</td><td>a sudden blowup<br>突然的发作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His blowup in the meeting shocked even his closest colleagues.  他在会上的爆发连最亲近的同事都震惊了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 explosion</td><td>/ɪkˈsploʊʒən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>爆发；爆炸</td><td>an explosion of laughter<br>爆发的笑声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her joke caused an explosion of laughter in the crowded hall.  她的玩笑在挤满人的大厅里引起一阵爆笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fit</td><td>/ˈfɪt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>相符，适合；合适的</td><td>fit the description<br>符合描述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The suspect they arrested did not fit the witness's description.  他们逮捕的嫌疑人并不符合证人的描述。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pacification</td><td>/ˌpæsəfəˈkeɪʃən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>平息，安抚</td><td>the pacification of the crowd<br>对人群的安抚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Hours of patient pacification finally calmed the angry crowd.  数小时耐心的安抚终于让愤怒的人群平静下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>taper</b></td><td>/ˈteɪpər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>逐渐减少，减弱</td><td>taper off gradually<br>逐渐减弱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The storm tapered off gradually as the evening wore on.  随着夜色渐深，暴风雨逐渐平息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 subside</td><td>/səbˈsaɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>减弱，平息；下沉</td><td>the pain finally subsided<br>疼痛终于减轻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The noise subsided as soon as the teacher walked in.  老师一进来，喧闹声就平息了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 die down</td><td>/ˈdaɪ ˌdaʊn/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>逐渐减弱，平息</td><td>let the noise die down<br>让噪音渐渐平息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They waited for the wind to die down before setting sail.  他们等风渐渐减弱后才起航。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 phase down</td><td>/ˈfeɪz ˌdaʊn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>逐步缩减</td><td>phase down coal use<br>逐步减少用煤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The country agreed to phase down coal use over ten years.  该国同意在十年内逐步减少用煤。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fall away</td><td>/ˈfɔl əˈweɪ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>逐渐减少，渐渐消失</td><td>fall away sharply<br>急剧减少</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Public support fell away sharply after the scandal broke.  丑闻曝光后公众支持急剧减少。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 let up</td><td>/lɛt ˈʌp/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>减弱，停止</td><td>the rain let up<br>雨停了</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rain finally let up just before the parade was cancelled.  游行取消前雨终于停了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>tardy</b></td><td>/ˈtɑːrdi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>缓慢的，迟缓的</td><td>a tardy response<br>迟缓的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company's tardy response angered customers waiting for their refunds.  公司迟缓的回应让等退款的顾客很生气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crawling</td><td>/ˈkrɔːlɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>缓慢爬行的，龟速的</td><td>crawling traffic<br>龟速的车流</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We sat in crawling traffic for nearly two hours.  我们在龟速车流中坐了近两个小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 creeping</td><td>/ˈkriːpɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>缓慢移动的，蔓延的</td><td>a creeping pace<br>缓慢的速度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The reform advanced at a creeping pace for years.  这项改革多年来以极缓慢的速度推进。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dallying</td><td>/ˈdæliɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>闲混的，磨蹭的</td><td>a dallying manner<br>磨蹭的样子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His dallying approach to deadlines infuriated the whole team.  他对截止日期磨蹭的态度让整个团队恼火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dawdling</td><td>/ˈdɔːdlɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>拖拖拉拉的，磨蹭的</td><td>a dawdling pace<br>拖拖拉拉的步调</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their dawdling progress meant the report missed the deadline again.  他们拖沓的进度使报告又一次错过了截止日期。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dilatory</td><td>/ˈdɪlətɔːri/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>拖延的，磨蹭的</td><td>dilatory tactics<br>拖延的手段</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His dilatory response to the complaint annoyed every member of the committee.  他对投诉迟迟不作回应，令委员会每位成员恼火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dragging</td><td>/ˈdræɡɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>拖沓的，缓慢的</td><td>a dragging negotiation<br>拖沓的谈判</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dragging trial exhausted witnesses on both sides.  拖沓的审判耗尽了双方证人的精力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lagging</td><td>/ˈlæɡɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>落后的，迟缓的</td><td>lagging sales figures<br>落后的销售数字</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Managers worried about the lagging performance of two branches.  经理们担心两家分店迟缓的业绩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 languid</td><td>/ˈlæŋɡwɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>没精打采的，虚弱的</td><td>a languid summer afternoon<br>慢悠悠的夏日午后</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A languid summer afternoon passed with nobody willing to leave the shade.  一个慢悠悠的夏日午后过去了，没人愿意离开树荫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sluggish</td><td>/ˈslʌɡɪʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>行动迟缓的，懒散的</td><td>feel sluggish all morning<br>整个上午都懒洋洋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He felt sluggish all morning and could barely finish his simplest chores.  他整个上午都懒洋洋的，连最简单的杂事都几乎做不完。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bolting</td><td>/ˈboʊltɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>飞奔的，突然疾冲的</td><td>a bolting horse<br>狂奔的马</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A bolting horse dragged the cart across the yard.  一匹狂奔的马把车拖过了院子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 brisk</td><td>/brɪsk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>充满生机的，有活力的</td><td>a brisk walk<br>快步散步</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A brisk walk before breakfast leaves me alert all morning.  早餐前快走一趟能让我整个上午都很清醒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fleet</td><td>/fliːt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迅速的</td><td>fleet of foot<br>脚步迅捷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fleet young messenger crossed the valley before dark.  这位迅捷的年轻信使天黑前穿过了山谷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 flying</td><td>/ˈflaɪɪŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>匆忙的；飞行的</td><td>a flying visit<br>匆匆一趟拜访</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She paid us a flying visit between two long meetings.  她在两场长会之间匆匆来看了我们一趟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 meteoric</td><td>/ˌmiːtiˈɔːrɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>流星般迅速而短暂的</td><td>a meteoric rise<br>迅速而短暂的崛起</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her meteoric rise in the company ended just as suddenly as it began.  她在公司里的迅速崛起结束得和开始时一样突然。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rocketing</td><td>/ˈrɑːkətɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>飞涨的，猛升的</td><td>rocketing housing prices<br>飞涨的房价</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rocketing fuel costs forced the airline to cancel routes.  飞涨的燃油成本迫使航空公司取消航线。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 scooting</td><td>/ˈskutɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>飞奔的，快速移动的</td><td>scooting across the yard<br>飞快穿过院子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A scooting squirrel crossed the path before we could raise the camera.  一只飞奔的松鼠在我们举起相机前穿过了小路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 scudding</del></td><td>/ˈskʌdɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>疾行的，飞驰的</td><td>scudding clouds<br>疾飞的云</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Scudding clouds raced across the moon all through the night.  疾飞的云整夜掠过月亮。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 scurrying</td><td>/ˈskɜːriɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>急匆匆的，疾走的</td><td>scurrying footsteps<br>急匆匆的脚步声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We heard scurrying mice behind the thin kitchen wall.  我们听见薄薄的厨房墙后有老鼠急窜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 swift</td><td>/ˈswɪft/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迅速的，敏捷的</td><td>a swift response<br>迅速的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her swift decision saved the patient a great deal of pain.  她迅速的决定让病人少受许多痛苦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 whirling</td><td>/ˈwɜːrlɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>飞速旋转的，急转的</td><td>whirling dust<br>飞旋的尘土</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Whirling dust hid the road from the drivers for several minutes.  飞旋的尘土让司机好几分钟看不见路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>tarnish</b></td><td>/ˈtɑːrnɪʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>玷污</td><td>tarnish one's reputation<br>玷污某人名声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One careless scandal tarnished a reputation built over thirty years.  一次不慎的丑闻玷污了三十年建立的名声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blemish</td><td>/ˈblemɪʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>缺点，污点；损害，玷污</td><td>a blemish on his record<br>他履历上的污点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One late payment left a small blemish on his credit record.  一次逾期付款在他的信用记录上留下了小小的污点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mar</td><td>/mɑːr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>破坏，削弱</td><td>mar the celebration<br>破坏庆典气氛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A careless remark at dinner managed to mar an otherwise perfect evening.  餐桌上一句轻率的话毁掉了本该完美的一晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stain</td><td>/ˈsteɪn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>沾污，玷污；污点</td><td>stain his reputation<br>玷污他的名声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scandal stained the family name for two whole generations.  这桩丑闻让这个家族的名声蒙污两代人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vitiate</td><td>/ˈvɪʃieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>削弱，损害；使无效</td><td>vitiate an agreement<br>使协议失效</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A single false statement can vitiate the entire contract.  一句虚假陈述就能使整份合同失效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>tasty</b></td><td>/ˈteɪsti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>美味的；令人愉悦的</td><td>a tasty dish<br>一道美味的菜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She cooked a tasty dish from the few vegetables left.  她用剩下的几样蔬菜做了一道美味的菜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 agreeable</td><td>/əˈɡriːəbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人愉悦的；相一致的</td><td>an agreeable companion<br>一个讨人喜欢的同伴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She found him an agreeable companion on the long train journey.  她觉得他是长途火车旅程中一位讨人喜欢的同伴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delectable</td><td>/dɪˈlektəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>美味的；令人愉悦的</td><td>a delectable dessert<br>美味的甜点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She served a delectable dessert made from summer berries.  她端上用夏日莓果做的美味甜点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delightful</td><td>/dɪˈlaɪtfəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人愉快的，宜人的</td><td>a delightful surprise<br>令人愉快的惊喜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We spent a delightful evening by the lake.  我们在湖边度过了一个愉快的夜晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 satisfying</td><td>/ˈsætɪsˌfaɪɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人满意的；有说服力的</td><td>a satisfying explanation<br>令人满意的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No one offered a satisfying answer to the reporter's question.  没人对记者的问题给出令人满意的答复。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 savory</td><td>/ˈseɪvəri/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>美味的，可口的</td><td>a savory dish<br>一道可口的菜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She served a savory pie made with mushrooms and herbs.  她端上一道用蘑菇和香草做的美味派。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 uninteresting</td><td>/ʌnˈɪntrəstɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无趣的，乏味的</td><td>an uninteresting report<br>乏味的报告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The food at the small hotel was cheap but completely uninteresting.  这家小旅馆的食物便宜，却毫无趣味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disagreeable</td><td>/ˌdɪsəˈɡriːəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人不快的；脾气坏的</td><td>a disagreeable task<br>令人不快的任务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cleaning the drains was a thoroughly disagreeable job.  清理下水道是件极其讨人厌的活儿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unpalatable</td><td>/ʌnˈpælətəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>难吃的；令人难以接受的</td><td>an unpalatable truth<br>难以接受的真相</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report contained several unpalatable truths about spending.  报告里有几条关于开支的难堪真相。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unpleasant</td><td>/ʌnˈplezənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人不快的，讨厌的</td><td>an unpleasant surprise<br>令人不快的意外</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was an unpleasant smell coming from the basement.  地下室传来一股难闻的气味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unwelcome</td><td>/ʌnˈwelkəm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不受欢迎的，讨厌的</td><td>unwelcome news<br>不受欢迎的消息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His arrival brought unwelcome news about the closing factory.  他的到来带来了关于工厂停产的坏消息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>tatty</b></td><td>/ˈtæti/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>破旧的，褴褛的</td><td>a tatty old coat<br>破破烂烂的旧外套</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wore a tatty old coat that had lost most of its buttons.  他穿着一件掉了大半扣子的破旧外套。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dilapidated</td><td>/dəˈlæpəˌdeɪtɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>破旧的，年久失修的</td><td>a dilapidated farmhouse<br>破败的农舍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They bought a dilapidated farmhouse and spent two years repairing it.  他们买了一栋破败的农舍，花了两年修复。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scruffy</td><td>/ˈskrʌfi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>邋遢的，破旧不整的</td><td>a scruffy pair of jeans<br>一条破旧的牛仔裤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He turned up in scruffy jeans and a torn grey sweater.  他穿着破旧的牛仔裤和一件破灰毛衣出现了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 seedy</td><td>/ˈsiːdi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>破旧的，寒酸的</td><td>a seedy hotel<br>破旧的小旅馆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They spent the night in a seedy hotel near the station.  他们在车站附近一家破旧的小旅馆过了夜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sleazy</td><td>/ˈsliːzi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>低劣的，肮脏破旧的</td><td>a sleazy nightclub<br>低档肮脏的夜总会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The band's first gigs were in sleazy clubs with broken lights.  乐队最初的演出是在灯坏掉的低档酒吧里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 tatterdemalion</del></td><td>/ˌtætərdɪˈmeɪljən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>衣衫褴褛的人；破烂的</td><td>a tatterdemalion beggar<br>衣衫褴褛的乞丐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A tatterdemalion figure waited silently outside the bakery door.  一个衣衫褴褛的身影默默等在面包店门外。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 threadbare</td><td>/ˈθredber/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈腐的；破旧的</td><td>a threadbare excuse<br>老掉牙的借口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He offered the same threadbare excuse for arriving late again.  他又用同一个老掉牙的借口解释迟到。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 smart</td><td>/ˈsmɑːrt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>聪明的；时髦的</td><td>a smart move<br>明智之举</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Buying the tickets early was a very smart decision.  提前买票是个非常明智的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>taunt</b></td><td>/tɔːnt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>嘲弄，讥讽；挑衅</td><td>taunt the loser<br>嘲弄失败者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The winning boys taunted the loser until a teacher intervened.  获胜的男孩们不停嘲弄输者，直到老师出面干预。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bait</td><td>/beɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>故意惹恼，戏弄；引诱</td><td>bait him about his accent<br>拿他的口音戏弄他</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The older boys baited him about his accent until he cried.  大孩子们拿他的口音戏弄他，直到他哭了出来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hassle</td><td>/ˈhæsəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>争吵；麻烦事</td><td>a hassle over money<br>为钱的一场争吵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They had a long hassle over who should pay.  他们为该谁付钱争吵了很久。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 haze</td><td>/ˈheɪz/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺侮，戏弄（新人）</td><td>haze the new recruits<br>戏弄新兵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Older members used to haze the new recruits for a whole week.  老成员过去会整整一周戏弄新来的成员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 heckle</td><td>/ˈhekəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>起哄，使难堪</td><td>heckle the speaker<br>向演讲者起哄</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Protesters began to heckle the speaker throughout the entire rally.  抗议者在整场集会上不断起哄质问演讲者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 needle</td><td>/ˈniːdəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>不断刺激，挑逗嘲弄</td><td>needle someone about failure<br>拿失败挖苦别人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His brothers needled him about the lost game for months.  他的兄弟们拿那场败局挖苦他好几个月。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ride</td><td>/ˈraɪd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>不断嘲弄，苛责</td><td>ride someone hard<br>对某人不停挑刺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Editors sometimes ride young reporters about every comma in their first stories.  编辑有时会为新记者初稿里的每个逗号不停挑刺。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>taut</b></td><td>/tɔːt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>紧绷的</td><td>pull the rope taut<br>把绳子拉紧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The wire was so taut that it hummed in the wind.  这根线绷得很紧，在风中嗡嗡作响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lax</td><td>/læks/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>松懈的，不严格的；漫不经心的</td><td>lax security standards<br>松懈的安保标准</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Lax security standards at the warehouse allowed the theft to go unnoticed.  仓库松懈的安保标准使这起盗窃未被发觉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 loose</td><td>/ˈluːs/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>释放，放开；松的</td><td>loose the dogs<br>放开猎狗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The keeper loosed the hawk and watched it climb into the sky.  饲养员放开那只鹰，看它飞向天空。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 slack</td><td>/slæk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>松弛的；疏忽的，大意的</td><td>slack about deadlines<br>对截止日期松懈</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new manager was slack about deadlines and lost several clients.  新经理对截止日期很松懈，因此丢了好几个客户。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>tawdry</b></td><td>/ˈtɔːdri/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>俗丽的，花哨而庸俗的</td><td>tawdry decorations<br>花哨庸俗的装饰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Tawdry decorations covered the walls of the cheap seaside hotel.  花哨庸俗的装饰铺满廉价海滨旅馆的墙壁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 meretricious</td><td>/ˌmerəˈtrɪʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>艳俗的，俗气的</td><td>meretricious ornament<br>艳俗的装饰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hotel lobby was crowded with meretricious ornaments of gilded plaster and plastic.  酒店大堂里堆满了镀金石膏和塑料做的艳俗装饰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gaudy</td><td>/ˈɡɔːdi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>俗丽的</td><td>gaudy jewelry<br>俗丽的首饰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She wore gaudy jewelry that clashed with her simple dress.  她戴着与朴素连衣裙不搭的俗丽首饰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flashy</td><td>/ˈflæʃi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>浮华的，华而不实的</td><td>a flashy sports car<br>浮华的跑车</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He arrived in a flashy suit that everyone immediately noticed.  他穿着一身浮华的西装到场，所有人立刻都注意到了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 garish</td><td>/ˈɡerɪʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过于鲜艳的，过于张扬的</td><td>garish colors<br>过于鲜艳的颜色</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lobby was painted in garish colors that hurt the eyes.  大堂被漆成刺眼的过于鲜艳的颜色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 exquisite</td><td>/ɪkˈskwɪzɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>精致精巧的</td><td>exquisite workmanship<br>精致的工艺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tiny box showed exquisite workmanship in every carved detail.  这个小盒子每一处雕刻细节都显示出精致的工艺。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 7

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>taxing</b></td><td>/ˈtæksɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>繁重的，费力的</td><td>a taxing task<br>繁重的任务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Caring for two infants proved a taxing task for the young couple.  照顾两个婴儿对这对年轻夫妻是繁重的任务。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 arduous</td><td>/ˈɑːrdʒuəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以做到的，费劲的</td><td>an arduous journey<br>艰苦的旅程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The climb proved far more arduous than any of them expected.  这次攀登远比他们任何人预想的都费劲。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 burdensome</td><td>/ˈbɜːrdənsəm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>繁重的，累人的</td><td>burdensome regulations<br>繁琐的规定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new paperwork proved burdensome for small rural clinics.  新的文书工作对乡村小诊所来说很繁重。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exacting</td><td>/ɪɡˈzæktɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>严格的，苛求的；费时间花心思的</td><td>exacting quality standards<br>严格的质量标准</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The airline maintains exacting standards for every part it installs.  这家航空公司对安装的每个部件都保持严格的标准。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grueling</td><td>/ˈɡruːəlɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>累人的，折磨人的</td><td>a grueling schedule<br>折磨人的日程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team endured a grueling schedule of twelve games in three weeks.  球队熬过了三周十二场比赛这种折磨人的赛程。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 laborious</td><td>/ləˈbɔːriəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>费力的，艰苦的；勤奋的</td><td>a laborious process<br>费力的过程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Copying the manuscript by hand proved a slow and laborious process.  手抄这份手稿是一个缓慢而费力的过程。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 onerous</td><td>/ˈɑːnərəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>繁重的，费力的</td><td>onerous responsibilities<br>繁重的责任</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Caring for the archive proved more onerous than the volunteers had expected.  照管这份档案比志愿者们预想的更费时费心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 toilsome</del></td><td>/ˈtɔɪlsəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>辛苦的，费力的</td><td>toilsome labor<br>辛苦的劳作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Copying manuscripts by hand was toilsome work that ruined many eyes.  手抄书稿是毁坏许多人眼睛的辛苦活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 light</td><td>/ˈlaɪt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻微的，不严重的</td><td>a light meal<br>一顿便餐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He suffered only light injuries in the accident.  他在事故中只受了轻伤。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 easy</td><td>/ˈiːzi/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>容易的，不费力的</td><td>an easy question<br>简单的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The homework looked easy, but it took him three hours to finish.  作业看起来容易，可他花了三个小时才做完。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>tedious</b></td><td>/ˈtiːdiəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冗长乏味的</td><td>a tedious explanation<br>冗长乏味的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager gave a tedious explanation of the new filing system.  经理对新的归档系统作了冗长乏味的解释。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 drab</td><td>/dræb/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>单调的，无聊的</td><td>drab gray walls<br>单调的灰墙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They painted over the drab gray walls with something warmer and brighter.  他们把单调的灰墙涂上了更暖更亮的颜色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dreary</td><td>/ˈdrɪri/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>单调乏味的</td><td>a dreary afternoon<br>沉闷的下午</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We spent a dreary afternoon indoors watching rain streak the windows.  我们在室内度过一个沉闷的下午，看着雨水划过窗玻璃。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 Jading</del></td><td>/ˈdʒeɪdɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌倦的，使人生厌</td><td>a jading routine<br>令人厌倦的日常</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of jading paperwork dulled his early enthusiasm.  多年令人厌倦的文书工作磨掉了他早年的热情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 monotonous</td><td>/məˈnɑːtənəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>单调的，无变化的</td><td>a monotonous voice<br>单调的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His monotonous voice made the afternoon lecture almost impossible to sit through.  他单调的声音让下午的讲座几乎难以听完。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stale</td><td>/steɪl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈旧乏味的</td><td>stale jokes<br>老掉牙的笑话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His stale jokes made the audience shift restlessly in their seats.  他老掉牙的笑话让观众在座位上不安地挪动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stodgy</td><td>/ˈstɑːdʒi/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>古板守旧的，乏味的</td><td>a stodgy old textbook<br>一本乏味的旧教材</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The course relied on a stodgy old textbook nobody enjoyed reading.  这门课用的是一本没人爱读的乏味旧教材。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stuffy</td><td>/ˈstʌfi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>沉闷乏味的，古板的</td><td>a stuffy lecture<br>沉闷的讲座</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The stuffy lecture put half the audience to sleep.  那场沉闷的讲座让一半听众睡着了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 weary</td><td>/ˈwɪri/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>令人厌倦的；疲倦的</td><td>a weary routine<br>令人厌倦的日常</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The weary meetings dragged on with no decision in sight.  这些令人厌倦的会议一拖再拖，看不到任何决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 drudging</del></td><td>/ˈdrʌdʒɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>苦干的，单调乏味的</td><td>drudging office work<br>枯燥的办公室工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of drudging clerical work dulled his early ambition.  多年枯燥的文书苦工磨钝了他早年的雄心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 entertaining</td><td>/ˌentərˈteɪnɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有趣的，引人入胜的</td><td>an entertaining speaker<br>有趣的演讲者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is an entertaining speaker who never reads from her notes.  她是位从不照稿念的有趣演讲者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 absorbing</td><td>/əbˈzɔːrbɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>引人入胜的，吸引人的</td><td>an absorbing novel<br>引人入胜的小说</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I found her account of the voyage utterly absorbing.  我觉得她对航行的叙述极其引人入胜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 stimulating</td><td>/ˈstɪmjəˌleɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>激励的，令人振奋的</td><td>a stimulating discussion<br>一场激发思考的讨论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The seminar was the most stimulating class of the term.  这场研讨会是本学期最能激发思考的课。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 engaging</td><td>/ɪnˈɡeɪdʒɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有吸引力的，讨人喜欢的</td><td>an engaging personality<br>讨人喜欢的性格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His engaging manner made strangers feel comfortable within minutes.  他讨人喜欢的举止让陌生人几分钟内就放松下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 engrossing</td><td>/ɪnˈɡroʊsɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>引人入胜的，令人专注的</td><td>an engrossing story<br>引人入胜的故事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The documentary was so engrossing that nobody left early.  这部纪录片如此引人入胜，没人提早离场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gripping</td><td>/ˈɡrɪpɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>扣人心弦的，紧张的</td><td>a gripping thriller<br>扣人心弦的惊悚片</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The final chapter is gripping from start to finish.  最后一章从头到尾都扣人心弦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 interesting</td><td>/ˈɪntrəstɪŋ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有趣的，引人入胜的</td><td>an interesting proposal<br>一个有趣的提议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She raised an interesting objection nobody had anticipated.  她提出了一个无人预料到的有趣反对意见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 intriguing</td><td>/ˌɪnˈtriːɡɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>引人好奇的，有趣的</td><td>an intriguing question<br>引人好奇的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She raised an intriguing point that nobody had considered.  她提出了一个无人考虑过的有趣观点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 involving</td><td>/ɪnˈvɑːlvɪŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>引人入胜的，扣人心弦的</td><td>an involving story<br>引人入胜的故事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The documentary is more involving than any thriller this year.  这部纪录片比今年任何惊悚片都更扣人心弦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 riveting</td><td>/ˈrɪvɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>吸引人的，极迷人的</td><td>a riveting account<br>引人入胜的叙述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The documentary offers a riveting account of life inside the mountain villages.  这部纪录片对山村生活的叙述引人入胜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>teeter</b></td><td>/ˈtiːtər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>摇晃，摇摇欲坠</td><td>teeter on the edge<br>在边缘摇摇欲坠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company teetered on the edge of bankruptcy for several months.  公司数月来一直在破产边缘摇摇欲坠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 careen</td><td>/kəˈriːn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>摇晃疾行，横冲直撞</td><td>careen down the hill<br>摇晃着冲下山坡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The empty cart careened down the hill and struck a fence.  空车摇晃着冲下山坡，撞上了篱笆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dodder</td><td>/ˈdɑːdər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>蹒跚，颤巍巍地行进</td><td>dodder along the path<br>沿小路蹒跚而行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old man doddered along the path with a cane in each hand.  老人两手各持一杖，沿小路蹒跚而行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lurch</td><td>/lɜːrtʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>蹒跚</td><td>lurch to one side<br>向一侧猛地歪倒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bus lurched to one side and threw passengers against the windows.  公交车猛地向一侧歪倒，把乘客甩到窗户上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reel</td><td>/riːl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>感到眩晕</td><td>reel from the blow<br>被打得头昏眼花</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The boxer reeled from the blow and clutched at the ropes.  拳手被那一击打得头昏眼花，抓住了围绳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 balance</td><td>/ˈbæləns/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>平衡；使平衡</td><td>keep his balance<br>保持平衡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dancer kept her balance on a single toe.  舞者用一只脚尖保持平衡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dither</td><td>/ˈdɪðər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>紧张不安；犹豫不决</td><td>in a dither<br>慌乱不安</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was in a dither about which offer to accept.  他为该接受哪份工作邀约而慌乱不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 falter</td><td>/ˈfɔːltər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>蹒跚；犹豫，踌躇</td><td>falter in his resolve<br>决心动摇</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Mr. Garrison never faltered in his resolve to finish the project.  加里森先生完成这个项目的决心从未动摇。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hang back</td><td>/ˈhæŋ ˌbæk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>犹豫不前，踌躇</td><td>hang back from deciding<br>迟迟不做决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He hung back at the door, unsure whether to enter.  他在门口犹豫，不确定要不要进去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scruple</td><td>/ˈskruːpl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>（良心上的）不安</td><td>have no scruples<br>毫无顾忌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He felt no scruple about reading the private letters of his brother.  他对偷看弟弟的私人信件毫无良心不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 shilly-shally</del></td><td>/ˈʃɪli ˌʃæli/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>犹豫不决，磨磨蹭蹭</td><td>shilly-shally over a choice<br>在选择上犹豫不决</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Stop shilly-shallying and tell us which offer you accept.  别再犹豫，告诉我们你接受哪个方案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stagger</td><td>/ˈstæɡər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>摇晃地走，蹒跚</td><td>stagger to his feet<br>摇摇晃晃站起来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The exhausted runner staggered across the finish line.  疲惫的跑者摇摇晃晃冲过终点线。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vacillate</td><td>/ˈvæsəleɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>犹豫不决；摇动，摇摆</td><td>vacillate between two options<br>在两个选择间犹豫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Parents who vacillate between approval and refusal confuse their children.  在同意与反对之间摇摆不定的父母会让孩子困惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 waver</td><td>/ˈweɪvər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>动摇，犹豫不决</td><td>waver between two options<br>在两个选项间摇摆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wavered for weeks before accepting the job offer.  他犹豫了好几周才接受这份工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wobble</td><td>/ˈwɑːbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>摇晃，颤抖；犹豫不决</td><td>wobble on the ice<br>在冰上摇晃</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old table wobbles whenever someone leans on its left side.  只要有人靠在左边，这张旧桌子就会摇晃。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 stabilize</td><td>/ˈsteɪbəˌlaɪz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>使稳定，使平稳</td><td>stabilize the ladder<br>把梯子稳住</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two ropes stabilized the ladder against the shaking wall.  两根绳子把梯子在晃动的墙上稳住了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dive in</td><td>/ˈdaɪv ˌɪn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>果断投入，毫不迟疑地开始</td><td>dive in without hesitating<br>毫不犹豫地投入</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rather than debate further, she dived in and started cooking.  她没再讨论，直接动手开始做饭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 plunge in</td><td>/ˈplʌndʒ ˌɪn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>一头扎进，果断行动</td><td>plunge in at once<br>立刻一头扎进去</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He plunged in and repaired the wiring before anyone objected.  他一头扎进去，在有人反对前就修好了线路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>teetotalism</b></del></td><td>/ˌtiːˈtoʊtəlɪzəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>禁酒</td><td>practice strict teetotalism<br>严格戒酒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His teetotalism began after watching alcohol ruin his father.  在看到酒精毁了父亲之后，他开始彻底戒酒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 intemperance</td><td>/ɪnˈtɛmpərəns/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>无节制，纵酒</td><td>years of intemperance<br>多年的纵饮无度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of intemperance had ruined both his health and his savings.  多年的纵饮无度毁了他的健康和积蓄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>telling</b></td><td>/ˈtelɪŋ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有效的，显著的</td><td>a telling detail<br>意味深长的细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One telling detail in the letter revealed the writer's true motive.  信中一个意味深长的细节揭示了写信人的真实动机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 compelling</td><td>/kəmˈpelɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极具说服力的</td><td>a compelling reason<br>令人信服的理由</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gave a compelling reason for leaving the project early.  他给出了提前离开项目的极具说服力的理由。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conclusive</td><td>/kənˈkluːsɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>决定性的，无可置疑的</td><td>conclusive proof<br>决定性的证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tests were not conclusive, so the doctors ordered more.  检测结果并不确定，医生便要求再做检查。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 convincing</td><td>/kənˈvɪnsɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人信服的，有说服力的</td><td>convincing evidence<br>有说服力的证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave a convincing explanation for the missing money.  她对丢失的钱给出了令人信服的解释。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 forceful</td><td>/ˈfɔːrsfəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>强有力的，有说服力的</td><td>a forceful argument<br>有力的论据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She made a forceful case for reforming the outdated grading system.  她为改革过时的评分制度提出了有力的论证。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 persuasive</td><td>/pərˈsweɪsɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有说服力的，令人信服的</td><td>a persuasive speaker<br>有说服力的演讲者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His persuasive letter changed the committee's mind overnight.  他那封有说服力的信一夜之间改变了委员会的想法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 satisfying</td><td>/ˈsætɪsˌfaɪɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人满意的；有说服力的</td><td>a satisfying explanation<br>令人满意的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No one offered a satisfying answer to the reporter's question.  没人对记者的问题给出令人满意的答复。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inconclusive</td><td>/ˌɪnkənˈkluːsɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>没有定论的</td><td>inconclusive evidence<br>无定论的证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tests were inconclusive, so they ran them again.  检测没有定论，于是他们又做了一次。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 indecisive</td><td>/ˌɪndɪˈsaɪsɪv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>犹豫不决的；非决定性的</td><td>hopelessly indecisive<br>极其优柔寡断</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is too indecisive to lead the team.  他太犹豫不决，无法带领团队。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ineffective</td><td>/ˌɪnɪˈfektɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无效的，不起作用的</td><td>an ineffective treatment<br>无效的治疗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old medicine proved completely ineffective against the new virus.  这种旧药对新病毒完全无效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 uncompelling</del></td><td>/ˌʌnkəmˈpɛlɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>没有说服力的，不吸引人的</td><td>an uncompelling argument<br>缺乏说服力的论点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The evidence was real but the argument remained uncompelling.  证据是真的，但论证仍然缺乏说服力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unconvincing</td><td>/ˌʌnkənˈvɪnsɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无说服力的，难以令人信服的</td><td>an unconvincing excuse<br>站不住脚的借口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness gave an unconvincing account of where he had been.  证人对自己的行踪给出了难以令人信服的说法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unpersuasive</td><td>/ˌʌnpərˈsweɪsɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无说服力的</td><td>an unpersuasive defense<br>无说服力的辩护</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer's unpersuasive closing argument failed to move the jury.  律师毫无说服力的结案陈词未能打动陪审团。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>temerity</b></td><td>/təˈmerəti/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>鲁莽，冒失</td><td>have the temerity to ask<br>竟然冒失地开口询问</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He had the temerity to ask his boss for another raise.  他竟然冒失地向老板再要一次加薪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 audacity</td><td>/ɔːˈdæsəti/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>大胆无礼，鲁莽</td><td>the audacity of his demands<br>他要求之大胆无礼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She had the audacity to correct the professor in front of the class.  她竟敢在全班面前纠正教授。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brashness</td><td>/ˈbræʃnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>鲁莽放肆，无礼</td><td>youthful brashness<br>年轻人的鲁莽放肆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His brashness in meetings offended several senior partners.  他在会上的放肆无礼得罪了几位资深合伙人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 presumption</td><td>/prɪˈzʌmpʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>冒失无礼；假定</td><td>forgive my presumption<br>请原谅我的冒失</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was sheer presumption to speak for the whole committee.  代表整个委员会发言纯属冒失无礼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 presumptuousness</del></td><td>/prɪˈzʌmptʃuəsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>冒失，放肆自大</td><td>sheer presumptuousness<br>十足的放肆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Correcting his own mentor in front of the class showed remarkable presumptuousness.  在全班面前纠正自己的导师，显示出惊人的放肆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 circumspection</td><td>/ˌsɜːrkəmˈspekʃən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>谨慎，慎重周详</td><td>act with circumspection<br>谨慎行事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The diplomat handled the question with unusual circumspection.  这位外交官以异常谨慎的态度处理了那个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cautious approach</td><td>/ˈkɔʃəs əˈproʊtʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>谨慎的做法</td><td>take a cautious approach<br>采取谨慎的做法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The board favored a cautious approach to the risky expansion.  董事会倾向于对这项冒险扩张采取谨慎做法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 pusillanimity</del></td><td>/ˌpjuːsələˈnɪməti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>胆小，懦弱无志气</td><td>diplomatic pusillanimity<br>外交上的软弱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their pusillanimity allowed the aggressor to seize the border towns unopposed.  他们的懦弱使侵略者不受阻碍地占领了边境城镇。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>temporize</b></del></td><td>/ˈtempəraɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>拖延以争取时间；打哈哈</td><td>temporize to gain time<br>拖延以争取时间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The minister temporized for weeks rather than announce an unpopular decision.  部长拖延数周，不愿宣布不受欢迎的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>temperate</b></td><td>/ˈtempərət/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>（言行举止）有分寸的，有节制的</td><td>a temperate response<br>有分寸的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave a temperate reply to a very rude question.  面对无礼的问题她的回答很有分寸。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 abstentious</del></td><td>/æbˈstɛnʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>节制的，有节欲的</td><td>an abstentious lifestyle<br>节制的生活方式</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The monks led an abstentious life of prayer and labor.  僧侣们过着祈祷与劳作的节制生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abstinent</td><td>/ˈæbstənənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>节制的，禁欲的</td><td>remain abstinent from alcohol<br>戒酒不饮</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He stayed abstinent from meat and wine for ten years.  他十年间不吃肉不喝酒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 continent</td><td>/ˈkɑːntənənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>自我节制的，禁欲的；大陆</td><td>remain continent<br>保持禁欲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The monks were expected to be continent in all appetites.  僧侣们应在一切欲望上自我节制。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 self-abnegating</del></td><td>/ˌself ˈæbnəɡeɪtɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>克己的，自我牺牲的</td><td>self-abnegating devotion<br>克己的奉献</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her self-abnegating care for others left no time for herself.  她克己地照顾他人，无暇顾及自己。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 self-denying</td><td>/ˌself dɪˈnaɪɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>克己的，自我节制的</td><td>a self-denying habit<br>克己的习惯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His self-denying routine allowed no sweets at all.  他克己的作息完全不允许甜食。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sober</td><td>/ˈsoʊbər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>严肃的；节制的</td><td>a sober assessment<br>冷静的评估</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee offered a sober assessment of the country's economic prospects.  委员会对国家经济前景作出冷静的评估。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 immoderate</td><td>/ɪˈmɑdərət/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无节制的，过度的</td><td>immoderate drinking<br>无节制的饮酒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her immoderate praise made the young writer distinctly uneasy.  她过度的赞美让这位年轻作家明显不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 extreme</td><td>/ɪkˈstriːm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>极端的，极度的；极端</td><td>extreme views<br>极端的观点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His extreme views cost him most of his old friends.  他极端的观点让他失去了大部分老朋友。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 frivolous</td><td>/ˈfrɪvələs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻浮的；无关紧要的</td><td>a frivolous remark<br>轻率无谓的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge dismissed the frivolous lawsuit within a single morning.  法官在一个上午之内就驳回了这起无理的诉讼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>tempestuous</b></td><td>/temˈpestʃuəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>狂暴的，动荡不安的</td><td>a tempestuous relationship<br>剧烈动荡的关系</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their tempestuous marriage ended after only two stormy years.  他们剧烈动荡的婚姻仅两年就结束了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 cataclysmal</del></td><td>/ˌkætəˈklɪzməl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>灾难性的</td><td>cataclysmal consequences<br>灾难性的后果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dam failure had cataclysmal consequences for the valley below.  大坝溃决给下游河谷带来灾难性的后果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tumultuous</td><td>/tuˈmʌltʃuəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>喧闹的；动乱的</td><td>tumultuous applause<br>雷鸣般的掌声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pianist returned to tumultuous applause from the standing crowd.  钢琴家返场时全场起立，掌声雷动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 turbulent</td><td>/ˈtɜːrbjələnt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>动荡的</td><td>a turbulent period<br>动荡的时期</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They married during a turbulent period of war and economic collapse.  他们在战争与经济崩溃的动荡时期结了婚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 serene</td><td>/səˈriːn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>平静的，安详的</td><td>a serene smile<br>安详的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She watched the serene surface of the lake at dawn without speaking.  她默默看着黎明时宁静的湖面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>tenable</b></td><td>/ˈtenəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有据可依的，无懈可击的</td><td>a tenable position<br>站得住脚的立场</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That interpretation is no longer tenable given the newly discovered letters.  鉴于新发现的信件，那种解释已站不住脚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 defendable</td><td>/dɪˈfɛndəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>站得住脚的，可辩护的</td><td>a defendable conclusion<br>站得住脚的结论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only one of his three conclusions is really defendable.  他三个结论中只有一个真正站得住脚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 justifiable</td><td>/ˈdʒʌstəˌfaɪəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有正当理由的，说得过去的</td><td>justifiable criticism<br>有理有据的批评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her anger over the broken promise was entirely justifiable.  她因承诺被违背而生气完全合乎情理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 maintainable</td><td>/meɪnˈteɪnəbəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>站得住脚的，可坚持的</td><td>a maintainable claim<br>站得住脚的主张</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That claim is hardly maintainable once you check the original data.  一旦核对原始数据，那个主张就很难站住脚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 supportable</td><td>/səˈpɔːrtəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>站得住脚的，有依据的</td><td>a supportable interpretation<br>有依据的解读</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His reading of the poem is supportable but not the only one.  他对这首诗的解读有依据，但并非唯一。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sustainable</td><td>/səˈsteɪnəbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>站得住脚的；可持续的</td><td>a sustainable argument<br>站得住脚的论证</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Without new evidence the theory is no longer sustainable.  没有新证据，这一理论已经站不住脚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unjustified</td><td>/ʌnˈdʒʌstəfaɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无正当理由的</td><td>an unjustified attack<br>无理的攻击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The harsh criticism of her work was completely unjustified.  对她工作的严厉批评完全没有道理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unsound</td><td>/ənˈsaʊnd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可靠的，有缺陷的</td><td>unsound reasoning<br>有缺陷的推理</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The engineer judged the old bridge structurally unsound.  工程师判定这座旧桥结构不牢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 specious</td><td>/ˈspiːʃəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>似是而非的，欺骗性的</td><td>a specious argument<br>似是而非的论证</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His specious argument collapsed as soon as the data were examined.  一查数据，他似是而非的论证立刻站不住脚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 indefensible</td><td>/ˌɪndɪˈfensəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>站不住脚的，无法辩解的</td><td>an indefensible decision<br>无法辩解的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cutting the safety budget was an indefensible decision.  削减安全预算是个无法辩解的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fallacious</td><td>/fəˈleɪʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>谬误的，不合逻辑的</td><td>a fallacious argument<br>谬误的论证</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer exposed the fallacious reasoning behind the entire claim.  律师揭穿了整个主张背后的谬误推理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 8

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>tenacious</b></td><td>/təˈneɪʃəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>黏性强的；不屈不挠的</td><td>a tenacious opponent<br>顽强的对手</td></tr>
