<tr><td colspan="7">例 She proved a tenacious opponent who never conceded a single point.  她是个顽强的对手，从不让出一分。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dogged</td><td>/ˈdɔːɡɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>顽强的；固执的</td><td>dogged determination<br>顽强的决心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her dogged determination carried the project through two years of setbacks.  她顽强的决心让这个项目度过了两年的挫折。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 insistent</td><td>/ɪnˈsɪstənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>坚持不懈的，执意的</td><td>insistent on the truth<br>执意追求真相</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was insistent that every witness be interviewed again.  她坚持要求重新询问每一位证人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 persevering</td><td>/pərsəˈvɪrɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>坚持不懈的，有毅力的</td><td>a persevering researcher<br>坚持不懈的研究者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a persevering student finishes such a long translation project.  只有坚持不懈的学生才能完成这样漫长的翻译项目。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 pertinacious</del></td><td>/ˌpɜːrtnˈeɪʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>顽固的，固执地坚持的</td><td>pertinacious in her demands<br>固执地坚持要求</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pertinacious reporter kept calling until she got an answer.  这位执着的记者不停打电话，直到得到答复。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 negotiable</td><td>/nəˈɡoʊʃəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可商量的，可让步的</td><td>the price is negotiable<br>价格可商量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The salary is negotiable, but the starting date is not.  薪水可以商量，但入职日期不行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 vacillated</td><td>/ˈvæsəleɪtɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>犹豫不决，摇摆不定</td><td>vacillated for weeks<br>犹豫了好几周</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He vacillated for weeks before finally rejecting the generous offer.  他犹豫了好几周，最终拒绝了那个优厚的提议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>tendentious</b></td><td>/tenˈdenʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有偏见的</td><td>a tendentious account<br>带偏见的叙述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics dismissed the book as a tendentious account of the revolution.  批评者认为这本书是对那场革命的偏颇叙述。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 partial</td><td>/ˈpɑːrʃl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>偏袒的，偏爱的；部分的，不完整的</td><td>partial to his friends<br>偏袒他的朋友</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The referee was clearly partial to the home team throughout the match.  整场比赛裁判明显偏袒主队。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 distorting</td><td>/dɪˈstɔːrtɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>歪曲事实的，使失真的</td><td>a distorting summary<br>歪曲原意的摘要</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The headline gave a distorting picture of what she actually said.  那个标题歪曲了她实际所说的内容。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 subjective</td><td>/səbˈdʒektɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>主观的，个人感受的</td><td>a subjective judgment<br>主观的判断</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Beauty remains a subjective judgment no test can settle.  美始终是任何测试都无法裁定的主观判断。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 biased</td><td>/ˈbaɪəst/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有偏见的，偏向的</td><td>a biased report<br>有偏见的报道</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Readers complained that the coverage was clearly biased.  读者抱怨这些报道明显带有偏见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unbiased</td><td>/ʌnˈbaɪəst/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无偏见的，公正的</td><td>an unbiased opinion<br>无偏见的意见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We need an unbiased assessment of the school's performance.  我们需要对这所学校表现的公正评估。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unprejudiced</td><td>/ʌnˈprɛdʒədɪst/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无偏见的，公正的</td><td>an unprejudiced view<br>无偏见的看法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only an unprejudiced reader can judge the argument fairly.  只有无偏见的读者才能公正评判这个论证。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>tender</b></td><td>/ˈtendər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>温柔的，心软同情的；正式提出</td><td>a tender heart<br>一颗温柔同情的心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She has a tender heart and cannot bear to see animals suffer.  她心地温软，见不得动物受苦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 extend</td><td>/ɪkˈstend/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>延长，扩展</td><td>extend a deadline<br>延长截止期限</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They agreed to extend the deadline by two weeks.  他们同意把截止日期延长两周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 proffer</td><td>/ˈprɑːfər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>献出，提供</td><td>proffer an apology<br>主动致歉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He proffered an apology before anyone had a chance to complain.  还没人来得及抱怨，他就主动致歉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trot out</td><td>/trɑt ˈaʊt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>端出（老套说法）；炫示</td><td>trot out the same excuse<br>老调重弹</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Politicians trot out the same tired promises every election season.  每逢选举，政客们都端出同样陈旧的承诺。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 beneficent</td><td>/bɪˈnefɪsnt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>（对个人或社会）有益的</td><td>a beneficent influence<br>有益的影响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her long service had a beneficent influence on the whole village.  她多年的服务对整个村子产生了有益的影响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 benevolent</td><td>/bəˈnevələnt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>仁慈的，行善的</td><td>a benevolent employer<br>仁慈的雇主</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A benevolent stranger paid for the family's train tickets.  一位仁慈的陌生人为这家人买了火车票。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 benignant</del></td><td>/bɪˈnɪɡnənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>仁慈的，宽厚的</td><td>a benignant smile<br>慈祥的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old abbot listened with a benignant expression on his face.  老修院长面带慈祥的神情倾听着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 compassionate</td><td>/kəmˈpæʃənət/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有同情心的，慈悲的</td><td>compassionate care<br>充满关怀的照护</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A compassionate nurse sat with the frightened child all night.  一位有同情心的护士整夜陪着那个受惊的孩子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 softhearted</del></td><td>/ˈsɔftˈhɑrtɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>心软的，慈悲的</td><td>a softhearted judge<br>心软的法官</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The softhearted judge suspended most of the sentence.  心软的法官缓期执行了大部分刑罚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 withdraw</td><td>/wɪðˈdrɔː/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>取出（款项）；撤回，撤退</td><td>withdraw money from the bank<br>从银行取钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He stopped at the bank to withdraw money before the long trip.  他在长途旅行前去银行取钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 atrocious</td><td>/əˈtroʊʃəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>极坏的，极其残忍的</td><td>an atrocious crime<br>残暴的罪行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court described the killing as an atrocious crime.  法庭称这起杀人事件是极其残忍的罪行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 barbarous</td><td>/ˈbɑːrbərəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>野蛮的，凶残的</td><td>barbarous cruelty<br>野蛮的残忍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The invaders were remembered for their barbarous treatment of prisoners.  侵略者因对俘虏的野蛮对待而被人记住。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bestial</td><td>/ˈbestʃəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>野兽般的，残忍的</td><td>bestial cruelty<br>野兽般的残忍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel describes the bestial cruelty of the prison guards.  这部小说描写了看守们野兽般的残忍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 brutal</td><td>/ˈbruːtəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>残忍的；直白无情的</td><td>a brutal attack<br>残忍的袭击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The prisoners described the brutal treatment they received in the camp.  囚犯们描述了他们在营中受到的残忍对待。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 callous</td><td>/ˈkæləs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无同情心的，冷漠的</td><td>a callous remark<br>冷漠无情的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His callous joke about the accident shocked everyone in the room.  他拿这场事故开的冷酷玩笑震惊了房间里的每个人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cruel</td><td>/ˈkruːəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>残酷的，无情的</td><td>a cruel joke<br>残忍的玩笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was cruel to leave the dog out in the storm.  把狗留在暴风雨里太残忍了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fiendish</td><td>/ˈfiːndɪʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶魔般的，恶毒的；极难的</td><td>a fiendish plot<br>恶毒的阴谋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The detective uncovered a fiendish plan to poison the water supply.  侦探揭露了一个恶毒的投毒供水计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inhuman</td><td>/ˌɪnˈhjuːmən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>残忍的，无人性的</td><td>inhuman treatment<br>残忍的对待</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report described inhuman treatment inside the camp.  报告描述了营地内残忍的对待。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 insensate</td><td>/ɪnˈsenseɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无感觉的；无理性的</td><td>insensate to pain<br>对疼痛毫无感觉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He lay insensate on the floor for several minutes.  他失去知觉地在地板上躺了好几分钟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sadistic</td><td>/səˈdɪstɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>残虐的，以施虐为乐的</td><td>a sadistic pleasure<br>施虐般的快感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He took a sadistic pleasure in reading out every low score.  他念出每个低分时有一种施虐般的快感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 savage</td><td>/ˈsævədʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>凶残的，野蛮的；猛烈攻击</td><td>a savage beating<br>野蛮的殴打</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics savaged the film for its careless treatment of history.  批评家猛烈批评该片对历史的草率处理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lent</td><td>/ˈlent/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>truculent 的后半（残片）</td><td>lent in truculent<br>lent 在词尾</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Here the word truculent is split into trucu- and lent.  这里 truculent 一词被拆成 trucu- 和 lent。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 uncompassionate</del></td><td>/ˌʌnkəmˈpæʃənɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无同情心的，冷酷的</td><td>an uncompassionate response<br>毫无同情的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His uncompassionate reply left the grieving family speechless.  他毫无同情的回答让悲痛的家人无话可说。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 vicious</td><td>/ˈvɪʃəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>凶恶的，恶毒的</td><td>a vicious circle<br>恶性循环</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dog gave a vicious bite that required several stitches.  那只狗凶恶地咬了一口，需要缝几针。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>tenuous</b></td><td>/ˈtenjuəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>站不住脚的，脆弱的；纤细的</td><td>a tenuous argument<br>站不住脚的论点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer's case rested on a tenuous argument about intent.  律师的诉讼建立在关于动机的站不住脚的论点上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 substantial</td><td>/səbˈstænʃl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>大量的，可观的；实质的</td><td>a substantial increase<br>大幅的增长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company reported a substantial increase in profits this quarter.  公司报告本季度利润大幅增长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>tepid</b></td><td>/ˈtepɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不太热心的</td><td>a tepid response<br>不热情的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The proposal met a tepid response from the board members.  提案得到董事们不热情的回应。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lukewarm</td><td>/ˈluːkwɔːrm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不热情的；温热的</td><td>a lukewarm response<br>冷淡的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The proposal met a lukewarm response from the whole committee.  这项提案在整个委员会得到冷淡的回应。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 halfhearted</td><td>/ˌhæfˈhɑːrtɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不感兴趣的，不热情的</td><td>a halfhearted attempt<br>不情不愿的尝试</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His halfhearted apology satisfied nobody in the crowded room.  他不情不愿的道歉没让挤满一屋的人满意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 uneager</del></td><td>/ʌnˈiːɡər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不热切的，不情愿的</td><td>uneager to volunteer<br>不愿主动报名</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team was oddly uneager to take on the new project.  团队对接手新项目出奇地不积极。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unenthusiastic</td><td>/ˌʌnɪnθuːziˈæstɪk/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不热情的，兴趣不大的</td><td>an unenthusiastic response<br>反应冷淡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His unenthusiastic nod told us he disliked the idea.  他不情不愿的点头说明他不喜欢这个主意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ardent</td><td>/ˈɑːrdənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>热情的，热切的</td><td>an ardent supporter<br>热情的支持者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He remained an ardent fan of the team for fifty years.  他五十年来一直是这支球队的狂热球迷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ebullient</td><td>/ɪˈbʌliənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>热情洋溢的，兴高采烈的</td><td>an ebullient host<br>热情洋溢的主人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An ebullient host greeted every guest at the door with genuine delight.  热情洋溢的主人在门口由衷欢喜地迎接每位客人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 feverish</td><td>/ˈfiːvərɪʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>狂热的；发烧的</td><td>feverish activity<br>狂热的忙乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was feverish activity backstage an hour before curtain.  开演前一小时后台一片狂热忙乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 keen</td><td>/ˈkiːn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>敏锐的，锐利的</td><td>a keen eye for detail<br>对细节的敏锐眼光</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The photographer has a keen eye for unusual light.  这位摄影师对特别的光线有敏锐的眼力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 passionate</td><td>/ˈpæʃənət/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>热情的，充满激情的</td><td>a passionate defense of art<br>对艺术的热情辩护</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is passionate about protecting the city's old neighborhoods.  她对保护城市的老街区充满热情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wholehearted</td><td>/ˈhoʊlˌhɑːrtɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>全心全意的，热情的</td><td>wholehearted support<br>全心全意的支持</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The plan won wholehearted support from every teacher present.  该计划得到在场每位老师的全力支持。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>terminology</b></td><td>/ˌtɜːrməˈnɑːlədʒi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>专业术语</td><td>medical terminology<br>医学术语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Medical terminology often confuses patients reading their own records.  医学术语常让病人看不懂自己的病历。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 argot</td><td>/ˈɑːrɡət/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>行话，隐语</td><td>thieves' argot<br>盗贼的隐语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel is thick with the argot of dockworkers.  这部小说充满码头工人的行话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cant</td><td>/ˈkænt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>行话；伪善之言</td><td>political cant<br>政治套话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His speech was pure cant dressed up as principle.  他的演讲不过是伪装成原则的套话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dialect</td><td>/ˈdaɪəˌlekt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>方言，土语</td><td>a local dialect<br>当地方言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her grandmother still speaks a dialect few young people understand.  她祖母仍说着一种少有年轻人懂的方言。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jargon</td><td>/ˈdʒɑːrɡən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>行业术语</td><td>technical jargon<br>专业术语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report was buried in technical jargon that ordinary readers could not follow.  这份报告埋没在普通读者无法理解的专业术语里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jive</td><td>/ˈdʒaɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>开玩笑，胡扯</td><td>stop jiving me<br>别跟我胡扯了</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Quit jiving and tell me what actually happened last night.  别开玩笑了，告诉我昨晚到底发生了什么。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lingo</td><td>/ˈlɪŋɡoʊ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>行话，隐语；外语</td><td>computer lingo<br>电脑行话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I could not follow the lawyers' lingo at all.  我完全跟不上律师们的行话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 patois</td><td>/ˈpætwɑː/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>方言，土话</td><td>the island patois<br>岛上的土话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fishermen bargained in a patois blending French and English.  渔民们用混合法语和英语的土话讨价还价。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 patter</td><td>/ˈpætər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>喋喋不休地说；急促轻响</td><td>the salesman's patter<br>推销员的顺口话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The magician kept up a cheerful patter while shuffling the cards.  魔术师一边洗牌一边说着轻快的顺口话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slang</td><td>/ˈslæŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>俚语，俗语</td><td>street slang<br>街头俚语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel is full of street slang that dates it precisely.  这部小说满是街头俚语，正好标出了它的年代。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>terminus</b></td><td>/ˈtɜːrmɪnəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>终点，终点站</td><td>the railway terminus<br>铁路终点站</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old railway terminus has been converted into an art museum.  旧铁路终点站已改建成艺术博物馆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 outset</td><td>/ˈaʊtset/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>开端，开始</td><td>from the outset<br>从一开始</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 From the outset, she made it clear that she would not compromise.  从一开始，她就明确表示自己不会妥协。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sustain</td><td>/səˈsteɪn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>支撑；维持</td><td>sustain economic growth<br>维持经济增长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The country cannot sustain such growth without more energy.  没有更多能源，这个国家无法维持这样的增长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>traumatic</b></td><td>/traʊˈmætɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>创伤的；令人痛苦的</td><td>a traumatic experience<br>创伤性的经历</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Losing his home in the fire was a deeply traumatic experience.  在那场大火中失去家园是他极为痛苦的经历。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>terse</b></td><td>/tɜːrs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>简洁的，简明的</td><td>a terse reply<br>简洁的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His terse note said only that the deal had collapsed.  他简短的字条只说交易破裂了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 compact</td><td>/ˈkɑːmpækt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>契约，协议；紧凑的</td><td>sign a compact<br>签订协议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two nations signed a compact to protect the shared river.  两国签订协议保护共有的河流。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 compendious</td><td>/kəmˈpɛndiəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>简明扼要的</td><td>a compendious reference guide<br>简明扼要的参考手册</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wrote a compendious history of the city in ninety pages.  他用九十页写成了一部简明的城市史。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 laconic</td><td>/ləˈkɑːnɪk/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>简洁（以至于显得粗鲁或难以理解）的</td><td>a laconic reply<br>简短生硬的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His laconic reply told us nothing about what had actually happened.  他简短生硬的回答没有告诉我们实际发生了什么。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pithy</td><td>/ˈpɪθi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>简练的，言简意赅的</td><td>a pithy remark<br>精练的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her pithy summary captured the whole debate in two sentences.  她简练的总结用两句话概括了整场辩论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 succinct</td><td>/səkˈsɪŋkt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>简明的，简洁的</td><td>a succinct summary<br>简洁的总结</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her succinct summary covered the entire report in three short paragraphs.  她简洁的总结用三小段概括了整份报告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 circuitous</td><td>/sərˈkjuːɪtəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迂回的，不直接的</td><td>a circuitous route<br>迂回的路线</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We took a circuitous route to avoid the flooded highway.  我们绕了一条迂回的路以避开被淹的公路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 circumlocutory</del></td><td>/ˌsɜrkəmˈlɑkjətɔri/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迂回冗赘的，绕弯子的</td><td>circumlocutory replies<br>绕弯子的答复</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His circumlocutory answers hid a very simple refusal.  他绕弯子的答复掩盖了一个简单的拒绝。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 diffuse</td><td>/dɪfˈjuːs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冗长散漫的；扩散的</td><td>a diffuse and wordy essay<br>散漫冗长的文章</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His argument was far too diffuse to convince a careful reader.  他的论证过于散漫，无法说服细心的读者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 long-winded</td><td>/ˈlɔːŋwɪndɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冗长啰嗦的</td><td>a long-winded introduction<br>冗长的开场白</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The chairman's long-winded report emptied half the meeting room.  主席冗长的报告让会场走了一半人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 prolix</td><td>/ˈproʊlɪks/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>啰嗦的，冗长的</td><td>prolix legal prose<br>冗长的法律文字</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge criticized the prolix brief for burying its argument.  法官批评这份冗长的书状埋没了自己的论点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rambling</td><td>/ˈræmblɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>漫无目的的，散漫的</td><td>a rambling explanation<br>散漫的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gave a rambling answer that satisfied nobody present.  他给出散漫的回答，在场无人满意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 verbose</td><td>/vɜːrˈboʊs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冗长的，啰嗦的</td><td>a verbose explanation<br>冗长的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her verbose report could have said the same thing in two pages.  她冗长的报告本可以用两页说完同样的内容。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 windy</td><td>/ˈwɪndi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>冗长的</td><td>a windy speech<br>冗长空洞的演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His windy speech said very little in more than an hour.  他冗长的演讲讲了一个多小时却几乎没什么内容。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wordy</td><td>/ˈwɜːrdi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>冗长的，啰嗦的</td><td>a wordy introduction<br>啰嗦的引言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The editor cut the wordy opening by half.  编辑把啰嗦的开头删掉一半。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>testimony</b></td><td>/ˈtestɪmoʊni/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>证词；证明；证据</td><td>give testimony in court<br>在法庭上作证</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her testimony in court decided the outcome of the entire case.  她在法庭上的证词决定了整个案件的结果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 attestation</td><td>/ˌætɛˈsteɪʃən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>证明，证词</td><td>written attestation<br>书面证明</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The notary provided written attestation of both signatures.  公证人对两个签名出具了书面证明。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 confirmation</td><td>/ˌkɑːnfərˈmeɪʃən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>确认，证实</td><td>await final confirmation<br>等待最终确认</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We need written confirmation before we release the funds.  在放款之前我们需要书面确认。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 documentation</td><td>/ˌdɑːkjəmenˈteɪʃən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>书面证据，文件证明</td><td>supporting documentation<br>配套的证明文件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The claim was rejected for lack of supporting documentation.  该索赔因缺少证明文件而被驳回。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 substantiation</td><td>/səbˌstænʃiˈeɪʃən/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>证实，证据支持</td><td>offer no substantiation<br>提不出任何证实</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rumor spread widely without a shred of substantiation.  这个谣言广为流传，却没有一丝证据支持。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 testament</td><td>/ˈtestəmənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>证明，明证</td><td>a testament to her skill<br>她技艺的明证</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bridge still stands, a testament to careful nineteenth-century engineering.  这座桥依然屹立，是十九世纪精心工程的明证。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 validation</td><td>/ˌvæləˈdeɪʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>证实，确认</td><td>independent validation<br>独立的验证</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The result needs independent validation before anyone publishes it.  这个结果在发表前需要独立验证。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disproof</td><td>/dɪsˈpruf/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>反证，驳倒</td><td>clear disproof of the theory<br>对该理论的明确反证</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One careful experiment offered clear disproof of the old claim.  一个严谨的实验明确反驳了那个旧说法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 9

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>testy</b></td><td>/ˈtesti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>易怒的，暴躁的</td><td>a testy reply<br>不耐烦的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He grew testy when the reporters kept repeating the question.  记者反复问同一个问题时，他变得不耐烦起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 choleric</td><td>/ˈkɑːlərɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>易怒的，暴躁的</td><td>a choleric temper<br>暴躁的脾气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His choleric temper frightened every new employee in the office.  他暴躁的脾气让办公室里每个新员工都害怕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 irascible</td><td>/ɪˈræsəbl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>易怒的，暴躁的</td><td>an irascible old man<br>脾气暴躁的老人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The irascible coach shouted at the referee over every call.  这位暴躁的教练对每一次判罚都冲裁判大喊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peevish</td><td>/ˈpiːvɪʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>易怒的，爱抱怨的</td><td>a peevish complaint<br>不耐烦的抱怨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She answered in a peevish tone and refused to explain further.  她用不耐烦的语气回答，拒绝再解释。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 perverse</td><td>/pərˈvɜːrs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>反常固执的，任性的</td><td>take perverse pleasure<br>反常地感到快意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He takes perverse pleasure in arguing against every popular opinion.  他反常地乐于反驳每一个流行观点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 pettish</del></td><td>/ˈpɛtɪʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>易怒的，任性烦躁的</td><td>a pettish reply<br>烦躁任性的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave a pettish reply and slammed the door.  她烦躁地回了一句，然后摔上了门。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 petulant</td><td>/ˈpetʃələnt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>易怒的，爱发脾气的</td><td>a petulant remark<br>使性子的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The actor made a petulant remark and stormed off the stage.  那位演员使性子说了句话，然后气冲冲地离开了舞台。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 affable</td><td>/ˈæfəbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>和蔼的，温和的</td><td>an affable host<br>和蔼的主人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our affable neighbor greets everyone on the street by name.  我们和蔼的邻居在街上会叫出每个人的名字打招呼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 good humor</td><td>/ɡʊd ˈhjumər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>好脾气，愉快心情</td><td>keep one's good humor<br>保持好脾气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He kept his good humor even after the third flight cancellation.  即便第三次航班取消，他仍保持着好脾气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unable to irritate</td><td>/ʌnˈeɪbəl tu ˈɪrɪteɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无法惹恼（对方）的</td><td>unable to irritate the judge<br>无法惹恼那位法官</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer's taunts were unable to irritate the patient old judge.  律师的挑衅无法惹恼那位耐心的老法官。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 imperturbable</td><td>/ˌɪmpərˈtɜːrbəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>冷静的；不慌不忙的</td><td>imperturbable calm<br>泰然自若的镇定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pilot stayed imperturbable while the engine warning sounded.  发动机警报响起时，飞行员依然沉着冷静。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 patient</td><td>/ˈpeɪʃənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有耐心的，能忍耐的</td><td>patient with beginners<br>对新手有耐心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A good teacher stays patient with beginners who ask the same question.  好老师对反复问同一问题的新手仍有耐心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>tether</b></td><td>/ˈteðər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（用绳、铁链）拴系，束缚</td><td>tether a goat outside<br>把山羊拴在外面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The farmer tethered a goat outside the barn every morning.  农夫每天早上把山羊拴在谷仓外。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 detach</td><td>/dɪˈtætʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使分离</td><td>detach the coupon carefully<br>小心撕下优惠券</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Carefully detach the label before you put the jar in water.  把罐子放进水里之前先小心地把标签撕下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tear loose</td><td>/tɛr ˈlus/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>挣脱，猛力扯开</td><td>tear loose from the rope<br>从绳索中挣脱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The frightened horse tore loose from the post and ran off.  受惊的马从桩上挣脱后跑掉了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>theatrical</b></td><td>/θiˈætrɪkl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>做作的，夸张的，矫揉造作的</td><td>a theatrical gesture<br>夸张的姿态</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He raised his arms in a theatrical gesture of despair.  他张开双臂，做出夸张的绝望姿态。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dramatic</td><td>/drəˈmætɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>戏剧性的；夸张的；显著的</td><td>a dramatic improvement<br>显著的改善</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her dramatic gestures made the simple announcement seem like a tragedy.  她夸张的手势让这个简单的通知显得像一场悲剧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 histrionic</td><td>/ˌhɪstriˈɑːnɪk/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>夸张做作的</td><td>histrionic gestures<br>夸张做作的手势</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her histrionic response to the small mistake embarrassed the whole team.  她对这个小错误夸张做作的反应让全队难堪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 melodramatic</td><td>/ˌmelədrəˈmætɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>夸张做作的，煽情的</td><td>a melodramatic reaction<br>夸张的反应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her melodramatic sighs made the argument seem almost funny.  她夸张的叹气让这场争吵显得有点好笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 stagy</del></td><td>/ˈsteɪdʒi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>做作的，戏剧化的</td><td>a stagy performance<br>做作的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His stagy bow at the door annoyed the other guests.  他在门口做作的鞠躬让其他客人反感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 natural</td><td>/ˈnætʃərəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>自然的，正常的</td><td>a natural reaction<br>自然的反应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It is natural to feel nervous before a big exam.  大考前紧张是很自然的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 understated</td><td>/ˈʌndərˌsteɪtɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>含蓄的，不张扬的</td><td>understated elegance<br>含蓄的优雅</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her understated gray suit suited the solemn occasion perfectly.  她那身低调的灰西装非常适合这个庄重的场合。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 reserved</td><td>/rɪˈzɜːrvd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>内向的，缄默的</td><td>a quiet, reserved man<br>安静内向的男子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her father was a reserved man who rarely spoke about his feelings.  她的父亲是个内向的人，很少谈论自己的感受。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 restraint</td><td>/rɪˈstreɪnt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>克制，约束</td><td>show great restraint<br>表现出极大的克制</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She showed remarkable restraint when the crowd booed her.  人群喝倒彩时她表现出惊人的克制。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>therapeutic</b></td><td>/ˌθerəˈpjuːtɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>治疗的，有疗效的</td><td>therapeutic effect<br>治疗效果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Gardening had a surprisingly therapeutic effect on her constant anxiety.  园艺对她持续的焦虑有出乎意料的治疗效果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 curative</td><td>/ˈkjʊrətɪv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有疗效的，治病的</td><td>curative properties<br>疗效特性</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Villagers still believe the spring has curative properties.  村民们仍相信这处泉水有疗效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 healing</td><td>/ˈhiːlɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>治愈的，有疗效的</td><td>healing power of time<br>时间的治愈力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The doctor praised the healing effect of rest and fresh air.  医生称赞休息和新鲜空气的治愈效果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 officinal</del></td><td>/əˈfɪsɪnəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>药用的，成药的</td><td>officinal herbs<br>药用草药</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The garden grows officinal herbs once sold in every pharmacy.  这个园子种着曾在每家药房出售的药用草药。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 remedial</td><td>/rɪˈmiːdiəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>补救的，治疗的</td><td>take remedial action<br>采取补救措施</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The school offers remedial classes for students who fell behind.  学校为落后的学生开设补习班。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 restorative</td><td>/rɪˈstɔːrətɪv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有益健康的</td><td>a restorative sleep<br>恢复元气的睡眠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A long walk by the sea proved wonderfully restorative after weeks of stress.  数周压力之后，在海边长时间散步格外有益身心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>thorny</b></td><td>/ˈθɔːrni/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>棘手的</td><td>a thorny problem<br>棘手的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The council postponed a thorny problem about school funding again.  议会又一次推迟了学校经费这个棘手问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 catchy</td><td>/ˈkætʃi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>棘手的；易记的</td><td>a catchy question<br>棘手的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The examiner slipped a catchy question into the paper.  主考人在试卷里塞了一道棘手的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delicate</td><td>/ˈdeləkət/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>微妙的，精细的；脆弱的</td><td>a delicate distinction<br>微妙的区别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She has a delicate ear for the subtle differences between dialects.  她对方言之间的细微差别有很灵敏的耳朵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 knotty</td><td>/ˈnɑːti/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>复杂的，困难的</td><td>a knotty question<br>棘手的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The professor posed a knotty question that nobody could answer confidently.  教授提出了一个没人能自信回答的棘手问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prickly</td><td>/ˈprɪkli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>多刺的；棘手的</td><td>a prickly problem<br>棘手的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Water rights remain a prickly problem for the two provinces.  水权对这两个省来说仍是个棘手问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ticklish</td><td>/ˈtɪklɪʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>棘手的，需小心处理的</td><td>a ticklish negotiation<br>棘手的谈判</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two governments face a ticklish negotiation over fishing rights.  两国政府面临一场关于捕鱼权的棘手谈判。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>threadbare</b></td><td>/ˈθredber/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈腐的；破旧的</td><td>a threadbare excuse<br>老掉牙的借口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He offered the same threadbare excuse for arriving late again.  他又用同一个老掉牙的借口解释迟到。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 banal</td><td>/bəˈnɑːl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非原创的，陈腐的</td><td>banal remarks<br>陈腐的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film's ending was so banal that the audience laughed.  这部电影的结尾陈腐得让观众笑了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 cliché</del></td><td>/kliːˈʃeɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>陈词滥调，老套的说法</td><td>an overused cliché<br>用滥的陈词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Saying that time heals everything has become a tired cliché.  说时间治愈一切已成为老套的陈词滥调。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 commonplace</td><td>/ˈkɑːmənpleɪs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>平凡的；老生常谈</td><td>a commonplace observation<br>老生常谈的看法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Video calls have become commonplace in ordinary office life.  视频通话在日常办公中已很平常。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hackney</td><td>/ˈhækni/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>使陈腐，滥用；陈腐的</td><td>hackney a phrase<br>把说法用滥</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Advertising has hackneyed that word far beyond any real meaning.  广告已把那个词用滥到失去真正含义。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shopworn</td><td>/ˈʃɑːpwɔːrn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈腐的，老一套的</td><td>shopworn arguments<br>陈腐的论点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The candidate repeated shopworn arguments that convinced almost nobody in the hall.  那位候选人重复着陈腐的论点，几乎没能说服场内任何人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trite</td><td>/traɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈腐的，陈词滥调的</td><td>a trite observation<br>陈腐的见解</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speech relied on trite phrases that moved nobody in the hall.  那篇演讲满是陈腐的套话，没能感动会场里的任何人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>throng</b></td><td>/θrɔːŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>大量聚集</td><td>throng the streets<br>挤满街道</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Fans thronged the streets outside the stadium hours before kickoff.  开赛前几小时，球迷就挤满体育场外的街道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flock</td><td>/flɑːk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>聚集，集体行动</td><td>flock to the beach<br>成群涌向海滩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Holidaymakers flock to the beach as soon as school ends.  学校一放假，度假者就涌向海滩。</td></tr>

<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mob</td><td>/ˈmɑːb/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>蜂拥围住；乌合之众</td><td>mob the singer<br>蜂拥围住歌手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Fans mobbed the singer as she left the stage.  歌手离开舞台时粉丝蜂拥围住了她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 swarm</td><td>/ˈswɔːrm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>蜂拥，云集；一大群</td><td>swarm into the square<br>蜂拥进入广场</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reporters swarmed into the square before the announcement.  记者们在宣布之前蜂拥进入广场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>thwart</b></td><td>/θwɔːrt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>阻挠，挫败</td><td>thwart an attempt<br>挫败一次尝试</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Alert guards thwarted the escape attempt.  警觉的守卫挫败了这次越狱企图。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 baffle</td><td>/ˈbæfl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使疑惑</td><td>baffle the experts<br>使专家困惑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The strange symptoms continued to baffle the visiting experts.  这些奇怪的症状一直使来访的专家疑惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 balk</td><td>/bɔːk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词、及物动词</td><td>阻碍；不愿接受，拒绝</td><td>balk at the price<br>因价格而退缩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Buyers began to balk at the price after the third increase.  第三次涨价之后，买家开始因价格而退缩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 checkmate</td><td>/ˈtʃekˌmeɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使受挫，彻底击败；将死</td><td>checkmate his opponent<br>彻底挫败对手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A clever amendment checkmated the governor's entire plan.  一项巧妙的修正案彻底挫败了州长的全部计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 discomfit</del></td><td>/dɪsˈkʌmfɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使尴尬；阻碍</td><td>discomfit an opponent<br>使对手窘迫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The blunt question visibly discomfited the confident speaker.  这个直率的问题明显让自信的演讲者局促不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 advance</td><td>/ədˈvæns/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>前进；促进；预付款</td><td>advance a theory<br>提出一种理论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The army advanced slowly through the thick forest at dawn.  黎明时军队缓慢穿过密林前进。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cultivate</td><td>/ˈkʌltɪveɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>提升，加强；种植，培养</td><td>cultivate a friendship<br>培养一段友谊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She cultivated a wide network of contacts during her years abroad.  她在国外的岁月里培养了广泛的人际关系网。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 encourage</td><td>/ɪnˈkɜːrɪdʒ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>鼓励，激励；促进</td><td>encourage students to read<br>鼓励学生阅读</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Good teachers encourage students to ask difficult questions.  好老师鼓励学生提出有难度的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 forward</td><td>/ˈfɔːrwərd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>向前的；前部的</td><td>take a forward step<br>向前迈一步</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He leaned forward to hear the quiet speaker better.  他向前倾身以便听清声音轻的讲话者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 foster</td><td>/ˈfɔːstər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>促进，鼓励，培养</td><td>foster cooperation<br>促进合作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new program fosters cooperation between local schools and museums.  新项目促进当地学校与博物馆之间的合作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 further</td><td>/ˈfɜːrðər/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>促进，推动；更远的</td><td>further his career<br>促进他的事业</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She took the job abroad to further her research on glaciers.  她接受海外工作以推进她的冰川研究。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 nurture</td><td>/ˈnɜːrtʃər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>培育，培养</td><td>nurture young talent<br>培养年轻人才</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Good teachers nurture curiosity rather than simply rewarding correct answers.  好老师培育好奇心，而不只是奖励正确答案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 promote</td><td>/prəˈmoʊt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>促进，助长；晋升</td><td>promote economic growth<br>促进经济增长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The campaign aims to promote healthy eating among children.  该活动旨在促进儿童健康饮食。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 support</td><td>/səˈpɔːrt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>支持，支撑</td><td>support a proposal<br>支持一项提议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Most members voted to support the new proposal.  大多数成员投票支持这项新提案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 aid</td><td>/ˈeɪd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>帮助，援助</td><td>come to someone's aid<br>前来帮助某人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Neighbors came to her aid within minutes of the fire.  火灾发生几分钟内邻居们就来帮忙。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bolster</td><td>/ˈboʊlstər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>支持，增强</td><td>bolster his confidence<br>增强他的信心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A word of praise from her would bolster his confidence enormously.  她一句赞扬就会极大地增强他的信心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 abet</td><td>/əˈbet/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>帮助，教唆（做坏事）</td><td>aid and abet<br>帮助并教唆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was charged with aiding and abetting the robbery.  他被控协助并教唆抢劫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ment</td><td>/mɛnt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>foment 的后半（残片）</td><td>ment after fo-<br>fo- 之后的 ment</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Joining fo- and ment gives us the verb foment again.  把 fo- 和 ment 接起来又得到动词 foment。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 facilitate</td><td>/fəˈsɪlɪteɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使变容易，促进</td><td>facilitate the discussion<br>促进讨论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new airport will facilitate tourism throughout the whole region.  新机场将促进整个地区的旅游业。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>ticklish</b></td><td>/ˈtɪklɪʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>棘手的，需小心处理的</td><td>a ticklish negotiation<br>棘手的谈判</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two governments face a ticklish negotiation over fishing rights.  两国政府面临一场关于捕鱼权的棘手谈判。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 huffy</td><td>/ˈhʌfi/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>易怒的，动辄生气的</td><td>get huffy over nothing<br>为小事就生气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gets huffy whenever someone questions his figures.  只要有人质疑他的数据，他就生气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tetchy</td><td>/ˈtɛtʃi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>易怒的，脾气坏的</td><td>a tetchy old man<br>脾气坏的老头</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tetchy old man complained about noise every single evening.  那个易怒的老头每天晚上都抱怨吵闹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 thin-skinned</td><td>/ˌθɪn ˈskɪnd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>敏感易怒的，受不得批评的</td><td>too thin-skinned for criticism<br>太受不得批评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is too thin-skinned to work with such blunt editors.  他太受不得批评，无法与这样直白的编辑合作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 catchy</td><td>/ˈkætʃi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>棘手的；易记的</td><td>a catchy question<br>棘手的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The examiner slipped a catchy question into the paper.  主考人在试卷里塞了一道棘手的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delicate</td><td>/ˈdeləkət/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>微妙的，精细的；脆弱的</td><td>a delicate distinction<br>微妙的区别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She has a delicate ear for the subtle differences between dialects.  她对方言之间的细微差别有很灵敏的耳朵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 difficult</td><td>/ˈdɪfəkəlt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>困难的，费力的</td><td>a difficult climb<br>艰难的攀登</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The final section of the trail is genuinely difficult.  这条小径的最后一段确实艰难。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dodgy</td><td>/ˈdɑdʒi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>棘手的，靠不住的</td><td>a dodgy situation<br>棘手的局面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Driving that dodgy old van across the mountains worried everyone.  开那辆靠不住的旧货车翻山让大家都担心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 knotty</td><td>/ˈnɑːti/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>复杂的，困难的</td><td>a knotty question<br>棘手的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The professor posed a knotty question that nobody could answer confidently.  教授提出了一个没人能自信回答的棘手问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prickly</td><td>/ˈprɪkli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>多刺的；棘手的</td><td>a prickly problem<br>棘手的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Water rights remain a prickly problem for the two provinces.  水权对这两个省来说仍是个棘手问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 problematic</td><td>/ˌprɑːbləˈmætɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有问题的，成问题的</td><td>a problematic assumption<br>有问题的假设</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The data set is problematic because many records are missing.  该数据集有问题，因为许多记录缺失。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sensitive</td><td>/ˈsensətɪv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>敏感的，灵敏的</td><td>sensitive to light<br>对光敏感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This instrument is sensitive enough to detect the faintest vibration.  这台仪器灵敏到能测出最微弱的震动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spiny</td><td>/ˈspaɪni/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>棘手的，麻烦的；多刺的</td><td>a spiny political question<br>棘手的政治问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor avoided that spiny political question during the entire debate.  市长在整场辩论中都避开那个棘手的政治问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sticky</td><td>/ˈstɪki/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粘的，有粘性的</td><td>sticky fingers<br>粘乎乎的手指</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children's hands were sticky with melted chocolate candy.  孩子们的手被融化的巧克力糖弄得粘乎乎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 thorny</td><td>/ˈθɔːrni/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>棘手的</td><td>a thorny problem<br>棘手的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The council postponed a thorny problem about school funding again.  议会又一次推迟了学校经费这个棘手问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 touchy</td><td>/ˈtʌtʃi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>易怒的，敏感的</td><td>a touchy subject<br>敏感的话题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Money is a touchy subject in that family, so avoid it.  钱在那个家里是敏感话题，别提。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tough</td><td>/ˈtʌf/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>艰难的，费力的；坚韧的</td><td>a tough assignment<br>艰巨的任务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Editing that manuscript proved a tough assignment.  编辑那份手稿被证明是项艰巨的任务。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tricksy</td><td>/ˈtrɪksi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>难对付的；狡黠的</td><td>a tricksy question<br>难对付的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The exam ended with two tricksy questions about ancient trade.  考试以两道关于古代贸易的刁钻题目结束。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 imperturbable</td><td>/ˌɪmpərˈtɜːrbəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>冷静的；不慌不忙的</td><td>imperturbable calm<br>泰然自若的镇定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pilot stayed imperturbable while the engine warning sounded.  发动机警报响起时，飞行员依然沉着冷静。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>tiff</b></td><td>/tɪf/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>小争吵</td><td>have a tiff with him<br>和他闹小别扭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She had a silly tiff with him about the dinner plans.  她和他为晚餐安排闹了一场无谓的小别扭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 altercate</del></td><td>/ˈɔltərkeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>争吵，激烈争辩</td><td>altercate over money<br>为钱争吵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The brothers altercated over the will for the entire afternoon.  兄弟俩为遗嘱争吵了整个下午。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bicker</td><td>/ˈbɪkər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>口角，争吵</td><td>bicker over small things<br>为小事争吵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children bickered over the last piece of cake.  孩子们为最后一块蛋糕争吵起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 brabble</del></td><td>/ˈbræbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>吵嚷，无谓争吵</td><td>brabble in the street<br>在街上吵嚷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two vendors brabbled loudly about a few missing coins.  两个小贩为几枚不见的硬币大声吵嚷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hassle</td><td>/ˈhæsəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>争吵；麻烦事</td><td>a hassle over money<br>为钱的一场争吵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They had a long hassle over who should pay.  他们为该谁付钱争吵了很久。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 quarrel</td><td>/ˈkwɔːrəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>争吵，口角</td><td>pick a quarrel<br>找人吵架</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The brothers had a bitter quarrel about their father's will.  兄弟俩为父亲的遗嘱激烈争吵了一场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 quibble</td><td>/ˈkwɪbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>吹毛求疵</td><td>quibble over details<br>在细节上挑刺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They quibbled over the wording while the deadline slipped away.  他们在措辞上纠缠不休，截止日期却悄悄过去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 row</td><td>/raʊ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>争吵，争执</td><td>have a row with him<br>和他吵一架</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She had a furious row with her landlord about the unpaid repairs.  她和房东就未付款的维修大吵了一架。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scrap</td><td>/skræp/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抛弃</td><td>scrap the whole plan<br>废弃整个计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Managers decided to scrap the whole plan and start again from scratch.  管理层决定废弃整个计划，从头再来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spat</td><td>/spæt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>（小的）争吵</td><td>a spat over money<br>为钱的小争吵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The couple had a brief spat over money and then apologized.  这对夫妻为钱小吵一场，随后互相道歉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 squabble</td><td>/ˈskwɑːbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>为琐事争吵；口角</td><td>squabble over money<br>为钱争吵不休</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children squabble endlessly about who sits by the window.  孩子们为谁坐窗边没完没了地吵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 10

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>tightfisted</b></del></td><td>/ˌtaɪtˈfɪstɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>吝啬的</td><td>a tightfisted employer<br>吝啬的雇主</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their tightfisted employer counted every paper clip.  他们吝啬的雇主连回形针都要数。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mean</td><td>/miːn/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑贱的；吝啬的</td><td>a mean little room<br>简陋卑贱的小屋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The family lived in a mean little room above a noisy printing shop.  一家人住在嘈杂印刷店楼上一间简陋的小屋里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 miserly</td><td>/ˈmaɪzərli/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>吝啬的</td><td>a miserly tip<br>吝啬的小费</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He left a miserly tip after complaining about the service all evening.  他整晚抱怨服务，最后只留下吝啬的小费。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 parsimonious</td><td>/ˌpɑːrsəˈmoʊniəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>吝啬的，过分节俭的</td><td>parsimonious with praise<br>极少给出赞扬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The parsimonious landlord refused to replace the broken heater.  吝啬的房东拒绝更换坏了的暖气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 penurious</del></td><td>/pəˈnʊriəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫困的；吝啬的</td><td>penurious circumstances<br>贫困的境况</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spent a penurious youth in a crowded tenement.  他在拥挤的廉租房里度过了贫困的青年时期。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sparing</td><td>/ˈsperɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>节俭的，节约的</td><td>sparing use of salt<br>节省地用盐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was sparing with salt because of his doctor's strict orders.  遵医嘱，他用盐十分节省。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 stinting</del></td><td>/ˈstɪntɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>吝啬的，节省克扣的</td><td>stinting with praise<br>吝于赞扬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was stinting with praise but generous with advice.  他吝于赞扬，却乐于给建议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bounteous</td><td>/ˈbaʊntiəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>慷慨的，丰裕的</td><td>a bounteous host<br>慷慨的主人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their bounteous host refused to let anyone leave hungry.  他们慷慨的主人不让任何人饿着离开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bountiful</td><td>/ˈbaʊntɪfəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>丰富的，充裕的</td><td>a bountiful harvest<br>丰盛的收成</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The valley gave them a bountiful crop of apples.  这个山谷给了他们丰盛的苹果收成。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 charitable</td><td>/ˈtʃærətəbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>宽容的，仁慈的</td><td>a charitable view<br>宽容的看法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He took a charitable view of his student's careless mistake.  他对学生的粗心错误持宽容态度。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 freehanded</del></td><td>/ˌfriːˈhændɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>大方的，慷慨的</td><td>a freehanded donor<br>慷慨的捐赠者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A freehanded donor paid for the entire new wing.  一位慷慨的捐赠者出资建了整座新翼楼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 generous</td><td>/ˈdʒenərəs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>丰富的，大量的；慷慨的</td><td>a generous helping<br>满满的一份</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She poured a generous glass of wine for each guest.  她给每位客人倒了满满一杯酒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 liberal</td><td>/ˈlɪbərəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>思想前卫的；慷慨的，大方的</td><td>liberal social views<br>前卫的社会观念</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His liberal social views often surprised the older members of the club.  他前卫的社会观念常常让俱乐部里年长的成员感到意外。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 munificent</td><td>/mjuːˈnɪfɪsnt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>慷慨的</td><td>a munificent donation<br>一笔慷慨的捐赠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The university received a munificent donation from a former engineering student.  这所大学收到了一位工程系校友的慷慨捐赠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 openhanded</del></td><td>/ˌoʊpənˈhændɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>慷慨的，大方的</td><td>openhanded generosity<br>毫不吝惜的慷慨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The openhanded donor paid for the whole library wing.  这位慷慨的捐赠者出资建了整个图书馆侧楼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unsparing</td><td>/ʌnˈsperɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>毫不留情的；毫不吝惜的</td><td>unsparing criticism<br>毫不留情的批评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The review was unsparing about the actor's weak performance.  这篇评论对该演员糟糕的表演毫不留情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>timeworn</b></td><td>/ˈtaɪmˌwɔːrn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈旧的，老套的</td><td>a timeworn saying<br>老掉牙的谚语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He comforted us with timeworn advice about patience and hard work.  他用关于耐心与勤奋的老套建议安慰我们。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 banal</td><td>/bəˈnɑːl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非原创的，陈腐的</td><td>banal remarks<br>陈腐的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film's ending was so banal that the audience laughed.  这部电影的结尾陈腐得让观众笑了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 cliché</del></td><td>/kliːˈʃeɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>陈词滥调，老套的说法</td><td>an overused cliché<br>用滥的陈词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Saying that time heals everything has become a tired cliché.  说时间治愈一切已成为老套的陈词滥调。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 commonplace</td><td>/ˈkɑːmənpleɪs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>平凡的；老生常谈</td><td>a commonplace observation<br>老生常谈的看法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Video calls have become commonplace in ordinary office life.  视频通话在日常办公中已很平常。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hackneyed</td><td>/ˈhæknid/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈腐的，缺乏创新的</td><td>a hackneyed phrase<br>陈腐的说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speech relied on hackneyed images of struggle and triumph.  这篇演讲依赖于陈腐的奋斗与胜利意象。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stereotyped</td><td>/ˈsteriətaɪpt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>老套的，刻板的</td><td>stereotyped characters<br>刻板的人物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel is full of stereotyped characters and predictable plots.  这部小说充满刻板人物和可预测的情节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 threadbare</td><td>/ˈθredber/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈腐的；破旧的</td><td>a threadbare excuse<br>老掉牙的借口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He offered the same threadbare excuse for arriving late again.  他又用同一个老掉牙的借口解释迟到。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trite</td><td>/traɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈腐的，陈词滥调的</td><td>a trite observation<br>陈腐的见解</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speech relied on trite phrases that moved nobody in the hall.  那篇演讲满是陈腐的套话，没能感动会场里的任何人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fresh</td><td>/ˈfreʃ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>新颖的；新鲜的</td><td>a fresh approach<br>新颖的方法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team needs a fresh approach to this old problem.  团队需要用新颖的方法处理这个老问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 new</td><td>/ˈnuː/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>新的，最近出现的</td><td>a brand new car<br>一辆全新的汽车</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They moved into a new apartment near the river.  他们搬进了河边一套新公寓。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 novel</td><td>/ˈnɑːvl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>新奇的</td><td>a novel approach<br>新奇的方法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her novel approach to the old problem surprised even the senior researchers.  她对这个老问题的新奇方法连资深研究员都感到意外。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 original</td><td>/əˈrɪdʒənl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>创新的；最初的，最早的</td><td>an original idea<br>有创意的想法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her proposal was genuinely original, unlike the tired ideas we usually hear.  她的提案确实新颖，不像我们常听到的那些陈旧想法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unclichéd</del></td><td>/ˌʌnkliːˈʃeɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不陈腐的，新鲜的</td><td>an unclichéd ending<br>不落俗套的结尾</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics praised the film for its unclichéd dialogue.  评论家称赞这部电影不落俗套的对话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unhackneyed</del></td><td>/ʌnˈhæknid/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不陈腐的，新颖的</td><td>an unhackneyed phrase<br>一个新颖的说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her essay pleased the judges with its fresh, unhackneyed images.  她的文章以新鲜不俗的意象取悦了评委。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>timid</b></td><td>/ˈtɪmɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>胆怯的，羞怯的</td><td>a timid smile<br>怯生生的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave a timid smile and slipped into the back row.  她怯生生地一笑，溜进后排座位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fainthearted</td><td>/ˌfeɪntˈhɑːrtɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>胆小的，怯懦的</td><td>not for the fainthearted<br>不适合胆小者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The steep coastal path is not for the fainthearted.  那条陡峭的海岸小径不适合胆小的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fearsome</td><td>/ˈfɪrsəm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可怕的，令人畏惧的</td><td>a fearsome reputation<br>令人畏惧的名声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The boxer had a fearsome reputation long before this fight.  这名拳手在此战之前早已令人畏惧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scary</td><td>/ˈskeri/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可怕的，吓人的</td><td>a scary movie<br>恐怖电影</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rope bridge looked scary but felt perfectly solid underfoot.  那座绳桥看着吓人，踩上去却很稳。</td></tr>

<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 timorous</td><td>/ˈtɪmərəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>胆小的</td><td>a timorous little voice<br>胆怯的小声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The timorous student finally raised her hand to answer the question.  那个胆怯的学生终于举手回答问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tremulous</td><td>/ˈtremjələs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>颤抖的，胆怯的</td><td>a tremulous voice<br>颤抖的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She answered in a tremulous voice that barely reached the back row.  她用颤抖的声音回答，后排几乎听不见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 stalwart</td><td>/ˈstɔːlwərt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>坚定忠诚的；强壮的</td><td>a stalwart supporter<br>坚定的支持者</td></tr>

<tr><td>↳反 adventurous</td><td>/ədˈventʃərəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>爱冒险的，大胆的</td><td>an adventurous traveler<br>爱冒险的旅行者</td></tr>

<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 audacious</td><td>/ɔːˈdeɪʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>大胆的，（但往往）愚勇的；令人吃惊的</td><td>an audacious plan<br>大胆的计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The general approved an audacious plan to cross the river at night.  将军批准了一个夜间渡河的大胆计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bold</td><td>/ˈboʊld/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>大胆的，勇敢的；醒目的</td><td>a bold move<br>大胆的举动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was a bold decision to leave a secure job.  辞去稳定的工作是个大胆的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 daring</td><td>/ˈderɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>勇敢，大胆</td><td>an act of daring<br>大胆之举</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Climbing that face alone took real daring and long preparation.  独自攀那面岩壁需要真正的胆量和长期准备。</td></tr>

<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tint</td><td>/ˈtɪnt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>淡色调；给…着色</td><td>a greenish tint<br>淡淡的绿色调</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old photograph had acquired a faint yellow tint.  那张老照片已泛上淡淡的黄色调。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 suffuse</del></td><td>/səˈfjuːz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（色彩等）弥漫，染遍，充满</td><td>suffused with warm light<br>沐浴在暖光中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Warm evening light suffused the quiet room with a golden glow.  傍晚的暖光把安静的房间染成金色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 decolorize</del></td><td>/diˈkʌləraɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使脱色，漂白</td><td>decolorize the fabric<br>使织物脱色</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Strong bleach decolorized the fabric within a few minutes.  强漂白剂几分钟内就使织物脱了色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>tinker</b></td><td>/ˈtɪŋkər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>乱修，乱调整</td><td>tinker with the engine<br>乱修发动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spent the whole weekend tinkering with the old tractor engine.  他整个周末都在乱修那台旧拖拉机发动机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 diddle with</td><td>/ˈdɪdəl wɪð/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>胡乱摆弄，瞎调整</td><td>diddle with the settings<br>乱动设置</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Stop diddling with the settings or you will break the camera.  别再乱动设置，不然你会把相机弄坏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fiddle with</td><td>/ˈfɪdəl wɪð/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>摆弄，乱动</td><td>fiddle with the controls<br>乱动控制装置</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He fiddled with the radio dial until the static disappeared.  他摆弄收音机旋钮，直到杂音消失。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fool with</td><td>/ˈful wɪð/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>瞎弄，胡乱摆弄</td><td>fool with the wiring<br>瞎动电线</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Never fool with the wiring while the power is still on.  电源没断时千万不要瞎动电线。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mess with</td><td>/ˈmɛs wɪð/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>乱动，胡乱干预</td><td>mess with my files<br>乱动我的文件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Someone messed with my files and deleted half the folder.  有人乱动我的文件，删掉了半个文件夹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 toy with</td><td>/ˈtɔɪ wɪð/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>摆弄；不认真考虑</td><td>toy with the idea<br>随便想想这个主意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He toyed with the idea of moving abroad but never applied.  他随便想过出国，却从未去申请。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 twiddle with</td><td>/ˈtwɪdəl wɪð/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>摆弄，无目的地拨弄</td><td>twiddle with the knobs<br>拨弄旋钮</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She twiddled with the knobs until the picture finally cleared.  她拨弄旋钮，画面终于清晰了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 adjust</td><td>/əˈdʒʌst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>调整；使适应</td><td>adjust to a new job<br>适应新工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took her months to adjust to life in a new country.  她花了几个月才适应在新国家的生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 repair</td><td>/rɪˈper/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>修理，修补</td><td>repair the roof<br>修补屋顶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Workers repaired the roof just before the rainy season began.  工人们在雨季开始前修好了屋顶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>truism</b></td><td>/ˈtruːɪzəm/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>陈词滥调</td><td>a familiar truism<br>一句熟悉的老话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It is a truism that hard work alone rarely guarantees success.  仅靠努力很少能保证成功，这是句老生常谈。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 banality</td><td>/bəˈnæləti/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>平庸；陈腐的话</td><td>the banality of his speech<br>他讲话的平庸乏味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film offered nothing but banalities about growing up.  这部电影只提供了些关于成长的陈腐说法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bromide</td><td>/ˈbroʊmaɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>陈词滥调；镇静剂</td><td>a tired old bromide<br>老掉牙的陈词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His speech ended with a tired old bromide about hard work.  他的演讲以一句关于努力工作的陈腐套话作结。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 cliché</del></td><td>/kliːˈʃeɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>陈词滥调，老套的说法</td><td>an overused cliché<br>用滥的陈词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Saying that time heals everything has become a tired cliché.  说时间治愈一切已成为老套的陈词滥调。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 platitude</td><td>/ˈplætɪtuːd/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>陈词滥调</td><td>utter a platitude<br>说一句陈词滥调</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speech offered nothing but platitudes about hard work and dreams.  这篇演讲除了关于努力与梦想的陈词滥调外别无他物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 commonplace</td><td>/ˈkɑːmənpleɪs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>平凡的；老生常谈</td><td>a commonplace observation<br>老生常谈的看法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Video calls have become commonplace in ordinary office life.  视频通话在日常办公中已很平常。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trope</td><td>/troʊp/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>比喻；陈词滥调</td><td>a familiar literary trope<br>常见的文学套路</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lonely detective has become a familiar trope in modern crime fiction.  孤独的侦探已成为现代犯罪小说里常见的套路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>tirade</b></td><td>/ˈtaɪreɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>长篇抨击性演讲</td><td>an angry tirade<br>愤怒的长篇抨击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach launched an angry tirade against the referee's decision.  教练对裁判的判罚发起愤怒的长篇抨击。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 harangue</td><td>/həˈræŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>（发表）长篇大论</td><td>a long harangue<br>长篇的训话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager harangued his staff for twenty minutes about missed deadlines.  经理就错过截止日期的事对员工发表了二十分钟的长篇训话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 diatribe</td><td>/ˈdaɪətraɪb/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>长篇抨击性演讲</td><td>launch into a diatribe<br>开始长篇抨击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His speech turned into a long diatribe against modern popular music.  他的演讲变成了对现代流行音乐的长篇抨击。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jeremiad</td><td>/ˌdʒerəˈmaɪəd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>哀诉，悲叹式的抨击文</td><td>a gloomy jeremiad<br>阴郁的哀叹长文</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His column became a weekly jeremiad about modern manners.  他的专栏成了每周对现代风气的哀叹式抨击。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 philippic</del></td><td>/fɪˈlɪpɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>激烈的抨击演说</td><td>deliver a fierce philippic<br>发表激烈的抨击演说</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The senator's philippic against the bill lasted two full hours.  这位参议员抨击该法案的演说整整两小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rant</td><td>/rænt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>（尤指长时间的）训斥，责骂</td><td>go on a rant<br>大发一通牢骚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach went on a long rant about the team's careless defending.  教练就球队漫不经心的防守训斥了很久。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 eulogy</td><td>/ˈjuːlədʒi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>悼词；颂词，赞辞</td><td>deliver a moving eulogy<br>致感人的悼词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She delivered a moving eulogy at her grandfather's funeral.  她在祖父的葬礼上致了感人的悼词。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 encomium</td><td>/enˈkoʊmiəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>赞颂之词</td><td>deliver a warm encomium<br>致以热情的赞颂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor delivered a warm encomium at the retiring teacher's farewell dinner.  市长在退休教师的告别晚宴上致以热情的赞颂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 panegyric</td><td>/ˌpænəˈdʒɪrɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>赞颂之词，颂文</td><td>deliver a long panegyric<br>发表一篇长颂词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The retirement dinner ended with a long panegyric to the old professor.  退休晚宴以对这位老教授的长篇颂词结束。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tribute</td><td>/ˈtrɪbjuːt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>称颂，颂词</td><td>pay tribute to her work<br>对她的工作表示称颂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Colleagues paid warm tribute to her forty years of patient teaching.  同事们对她四十年的耐心教学致以热情的颂扬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>toady</b></td><td>/ˈtoʊdi/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>马屁精；拍马屁</td><td>toady to the powerful<br>对权贵拍马屁</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Junior officials toady to the powerful hoping for quick promotion.  下级官员对权贵拍马屁，希望快速升迁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sycophant</td><td>/ˈsɪkəfænt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>马屁精</td><td>surrounded by sycophants<br>被马屁精包围</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The king was surrounded by sycophants who praised his every word.  国王身边围着一群对他每句话都大加称赞的马屁精。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 groveler</del></td><td>/ˈɡrɑvələr/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>卑躬屈膝者，谄媚者</td><td>a shameless groveler<br>无耻的卑躬者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court was full of grovelers who feared the queen's temper.  宫廷里满是害怕女王脾气的卑躬屈膝者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flatterer</td><td>/ˈflætərər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>谄媚者，奉承者</td><td>surrounded by flatterers<br>被奉承者围绕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A wise ruler learns to distrust every skillful flatterer.  明智的统治者学会怀疑每个精于奉承的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>tonic</b></td><td>/ˈtɑːnɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>滋补的，有益健康的</td><td>a tonic effect<br>滋补的功效</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A week in the mountains had a tonic effect on her.  在山里待一周对她起了滋补强身的作用。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 medicinal</td><td>/məˈdɪsənəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有药效的，药用的</td><td>medicinal plants<br>药用植物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The forest contains dozens of medicinal plants still used locally.  这片森林里有几十种当地仍在使用的药用植物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 restorative</td><td>/rɪˈstɔːrətɪv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有益健康的</td><td>a restorative sleep<br>恢复元气的睡眠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A long walk by the sea proved wonderfully restorative after weeks of stress.  数周压力之后，在海边长时间散步格外有益身心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 salubrious</td><td>/səˈluːbriəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有益健康的</td><td>a salubrious mountain climate<br>有益健康的山地气候</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Doctors sent him to a salubrious mountain village to recover his strength.  医生把他送到有益健康的山村去恢复体力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 salutary</td><td>/ˈsæljəteri/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有益健康的；有利的，利好的</td><td>a salutary lesson<br>有益的教训</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The failed launch had a salutary effect on the careless habits of the team.  那次发射失败对团队的马虎习惯起了有益的作用。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 salutiferous</del></td><td>/ˌsæljuˈtɪfərəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有益健康的</td><td>salutiferous mountain air<br>有益健康的山间空气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Doctors once sent patients here for the salutiferous sea air.  医生曾把病人送到这里享受有益健康的海风。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 sanative</del></td><td>/ˈsænətɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有疗效的，治疗的</td><td>sanative herbs and springs<br>有疗效的草药与泉水</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Villagers long believed the spring had sanative properties.  村民长期相信这泉水有疗效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wholesome</td><td>/ˈhoʊlsəm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有益身心健康的</td><td>wholesome home-cooked food<br>有益健康的家常饭菜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children grew strong on wholesome food and plenty of fresh air.  孩子们靠有益健康的食物和充足的新鲜空气长得很结实。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 insalubrious</del></td><td>/ˌɪnsəˈlubriəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不利健康的，有害的</td><td>an insalubrious climate<br>不利健康的气候</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The damp, insalubrious basement made both children ill.  潮湿不卫生的地下室使两个孩子都病了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 noxious</td><td>/ˈnɑːkʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有害的，有毒的；产生强烈厌恶的</td><td>noxious fumes<br>有毒的烟气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Noxious fumes from the factory forced the school to close for two days.  工厂排出的有毒烟气迫使学校停课两天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unhealthful</td><td>/ʌnˈhelθfəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不利健康的</td><td>unhealthful working conditions<br>不利健康的工作条件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Inspectors closed the plant for unhealthful working conditions.  检查人员因工作条件有害健康而关闭了该厂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unhealthy</td><td>/ʌnˈhelθi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不健康的，有害健康的</td><td>an unhealthy diet<br>不健康的饮食</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of an unhealthy diet finally caught up with him.  多年不健康的饮食终于让他付出了代价。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unwholesome</td><td>/ʌnˈhoʊlsəm/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有害的，不健康的</td><td>an unwholesome influence<br>不良的影响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her parents thought the crowd an unwholesome influence.  她父母认为那群人是不良影响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
